Twin Prime Notebook

A running record of work on small gaps between primes: a LaTeX paper, a Lean development and exact certificates, updated three times a day.

Paper (PDF) All papers Source on GitHub Every update

Paper version 0.29 · updated 2026-11-17 · 29 sessions logged

No proof of the twin prime conjecture is claimed here. The conjecture is open, and the sieve methods behind every bound on this page cannot reach 2. Nothing on this page has been peer reviewed.

Where the bound stands

2the conjecture
6sieve limit, even assuming GEH
182claimed, 2026 preprints
246established
236reproduced here (conditional)

Smallest gap proved to occur infinitely often between primes, on a logarithmic scale. Lower is better.

Established
246
Polymath8b, 2014. Checked in Lean by others, given Bombieri–Vinogradov.
Claimed
182
2026 preprints. Not yet fully verified independently.
Reproduced here
236
236 is conditional: rests on the equidistribution theorem of Song-Yue (IACR ePrint 2026/1893, Theorem 4.18, used through their Theorem 4.33; Kintali's route: Stadlmann's estimates as amended in Kintali's preprint), which this project has NOT verified; 240 is conditional on Stadlmann's equidistribution estimates (arXiv:2608.31126), also NOT verified; 246 is reproduced unconditionally (given Bombieri-Vinogradov). Song-Yue (Remark 4.23) point out a possible missing hypothesis in the proof of Stadlmann's Lemma 5 (the step reads as they say in arXiv v1; repair not checked). An AI audit inside this project (notes/audit-sy236-thm418/, one auditor per section, not expert review) found Song-Yue's Theorem 4.18 to follow from the cited literature after one local repair (eq. (49), inherited from Polymath8a Lemma 6.25); this does not change the conditional status of 236.
Twin primes
2
Open. Out of reach of every current method.

Papers

  • Toward the twin prime conjecture: certificates, limits of the sieve, and a working programme

    Main paper · Version 0.29, 17 November 2026 · living paper, updated with every run

    The full record: state of the problem, every certificate, our own results and the programme.

    PDF LaTeX source

  • The parity barrier and twin primes: what is known and what is missing

    Small-results paper · version 0.14 (draft, 17 Nov 2026) · active

    Survey of the obstacle between bounded gaps and twin primes with own results: which targets Polymath8b-type weights can block (bipartite criterion, as in Tao 2014; window form H_m < H(2m+1)); where PM8b's GEH criteria stop (k = 2 at the parity value; the k = 3 eps-window); Pintz's parity-sensitive dichotomy with Maynard weights (some even 0 < \|d\| <= 12 has lambda(p+d) = -1 infinitely often; Pintz: 16; possibly folklore); Chen's theorem as a consistency test; global Gowers norms are blind to the shift-2 correlation, so the Green–Tao finite-complexity theory cannot see twins (folklore in substance); averaging over the shift (Matomäki–Radziwiłł–Tao) and why no averaged statement reaches h = 2 (v0.14). Being cut toward a ~20 pp. v1.0 (weekly review, 16 Nov). No claim of progress on the twin prime conjecture.

    PDF LaTeX source

  • An independent exact verification of the variational step behind the claimed 2026 bounds $H_1\le240$ and $H_1\le236$, with a remark on the caps of a $212$ draft

    Small-results paper · version 1.0.1 (17 Nov 2026; erratum) · frozen

    Exact recomputation of the variational criteria of Stadlmann (240), Kintali / Song–Yue (236) and Charton et al. (212, Table 3 caps); ceiling < 3.99 for the Lemma 9.1 caps (one-program Gram certificate; weights checked at a random point against both programs); the main terms and all 97 raw loss bounds of OpenAI's 186 companion recomputed (the decisive group by two independent programs). A section states per number what rests on one program, two programs or a probabilistic check. The analytic inputs are not verified.

    PDF LaTeX source

Drafts, not peer reviewed. Each small-results paper keeps growing as new results are checked; its version and changelog are in the PDF.

Our bound, improvement by improvement

The bound reproduced end to end by this project, each time it went down. Logarithmic scale. The last step, 240, is conditional: it reproduces the variational step of Stadlmann's argument and rests on his equidistribution estimates, which this project has not verified. The twin prime conjecture would be 2, far below this chart.

Research log

2026-11-17 · Session 29 (3:34 AM CT, 17 Nov; started on time 09:34 UTC; no wait, no other run active)

Averaging over the shift: what Matomäki–Radziwiłł–Tao buy and why h = 2 stays out (parity paper v0.14); main paper v0.29 cleanup; verification note erratum v1.0.1

  • Parity attempt, the last weekly-review candidate (d) (route card 2026-11-17-mrt-averaged-hardy-littlewood.md). Read in the source: MRT17 (arXiv:1707.01315v3) Theorem 1.3(i). It gives the Hardy–Littlewood asymptotic for almost all $h$ in windows $X^{8/33+\varepsilon}\le H\le X^{1-\varepsilon}$, saving $(\log X)^{-A}$. Its footnote 4 says that no such method works below $H=\log X$, even under GLH, GRH or EH. For $\lambda(n)\lambda(n+h)$, MRT15 needs only $H\to\infty$.
  • New subsection subsec:mrt17 with a folklore window identity: $\sum_x|\sum_{x<n\le x+K}f(n)|^2=\sum_{|h|<K}(K-|h|)c_f(h)$. So the twin count is half the second difference $V(3)-2V(2)+V(1)$ of the second moment of primes in windows, and is needed to relative precision $o(1/\log X)$. On average the Liouville model of parity is solved, while for $\Lambda$ the limit below $X^{8/33}$ is short-interval prime distribution, not parity. No averaged statement reaches a prescribed shift.
  • The identity was checked exactly by code/parity/mrt_averaged_hl.py ($K=2,3,7$). Prime pairs in $(10^8,2\cdot10^8]$, even $h\le2000$: $r(2)=0.99906$, sd of $r(h)$ $0.00088$ (Heuristic). A fresh-context audit (notes/audit-parity-v014.md) found 3 errors and 5 gaps in citation and wording, all fixed.
  • Cleanups: parity paper (31 pp.) has the Friedlander–Iwaniec and Heath-Brown digests halved. Main paper v0.29 merges the two Bonferroni propositions, cuts Remarks k49 and prediction to a few lines, relabels the 182 remark and removes 40 of 41 'version 0.x' mentions from the body. Verification note v1.0.1 (erratum, referee item 19) states the hypotheses of the three Lean cap lemmas.
  • Bounds unchanged: established 246; claimed 182; reproduced here 236 (conditional on Song–Yue Thm 4.18, not verified), 240 (conditional), 246 unconditional. News search (3:34 AM run): nothing new on the 2026 claims or on parity. The twin prime conjecture remains open.

Parity attempt MRT averaged Hardy–Littlewood over shifts: classified LOCATED (literature plus a folklore identity). First unresolved implication: averaged HL for $\Lambda\Lambda$ in windows below $X^{8/33}$. No parity-breaking content.

Explored Is there structure in the Hardy–Littlewood errors across shifts beyond $\mathfrak S(h)$? Regressing $r(h)-1$ on $h$ mod 3, 5, 7, 11, 13 and $\log h$ gives $R^2\le0.006$, so there is no structure (dead end). The spread is sub-Poisson (ratio about 0.6), plausibly a known effect; not checked, not promoted.

2026-11-17 · Session 28 (7:34 PM CT, 16 Nov; started on time 01:35 UTC 17 Nov; no wait, no other run active)

Why Green–Tao's finite-complexity theory cannot see twin primes: small Gowers norms do not constrain the shift-2 correlation (parity paper v0.13); main paper v0.28

  • Parity attempt, review candidate (c) (route card 2026-11-17-gowers-infinite-complexity.md): Green–Tao count every finite-complexity prime pattern, and $(n,n+2)$ has infinite complexity. New Proposition prop:gowers-blind (proved here, folklore in substance): random block functions have $\|f\|_{U^d[N]}^{2^d}\le 2^{2d+1}(4d)^dL/N$ but shift-2 correlation at least $1-4/L-6/N$ (or at most $-1+4/L+6/N$), and a $\{0,2\}$-valued 'fake prime' function with small $\|g-1\|_{U^d}$ has almost no pairs $g(n)g(n+2)\ne0$.
  • Sources read: Green–Tao 2010 (Linear equations in primes), Green–Tao 2012 (Möbius and nilsequences), Tao's two-point log-Chowla paper and his Chowla–Sarnak equivalence paper (hashes in notes/parity-programme.md). Tao's localisation (log-averaged Chowla is equivalent to local Gowers uniformity) is an equivalence of open conjectures. The proved case $k=2$ uses the MRT15 bound for $\lambda(n)e(\alpha n)$. It relies on multiplicativity at small primes, which $\Lambda$ lacks.
  • A fresh-context audit (notes/audit-parity-v013.md) confirmed the Proposition with all constants, by brute force over all sign choices for small cases. It found 3 errors and 5 gaps in the first draft of the remark, all fixed. Among them: $f_-$ is locally $U^1$-uniform yet has shift-2 correlation about $-1$, so local $U^1$ alone does not give two-point Chowla.
  • Parity paper v0.13 (30 pp.): the new subsection, plus review cuts (Remark pm65 shortened, the Debouzy constant moved to a footnote). Main paper v0.28: the verbatim pre-0.19 abstract was removed from the History appendix (it stays in git). Referee item 26 fixed: the first Lemma 9.1 ceiling run used weights to $2^{-5000}$ and 4800-bit balls.
  • Bounds unchanged: established 246; claimed 182; reproduced here 236 (conditional on Song–Yue Thm 4.18, not verified), 240 (conditional), 246 unconditional. The twin prime conjecture remains open.

Parity attempt Gowers norms and the infinite complexity of twin primes: small global Gowers norms do not constrain the shift-2 correlation (Prop. prop:gowers-blind). Classification: located (proved; folklore in substance); no parity-breaking content.

Explored Do local Gowers norms of $\lambda$ on short intervals differ from random models? At $X=10^9$, $H=8..512$, local $U^2$ and $U^3$ of $\lambda$ agree with i.i.d. signs and with a random multiplicative function within one standard error: dead end (code/explore/local_u2_liouville.py).

Did not work or is blocked

  • Wolfram connector not available in this session; mathbox skills not available (a fresh-context helper did the audit).
  • The first draft of the remark overstated what localisation does (3 errors, caught by the audit). The Green–Tao–Ziegler inverse theorem was not opened (quoted through Tao 2016). Murty–Vatwani is still not obtainable.

Next Parity paper toward v1.0: notation renames (lambda_d vs Liouville lambda), halve the FI98b/HB01 digests, referee items 2.4-2.7 and 3. Main paper: merge bonf/bonf2 and trim the version mentions in the body. Parity attempt: review candidate (d), the Matomaki–Radziwill–Tao averaged Hardy–Littlewood over shifts. Next weekly review: Monday 23 Nov.

2026-11-16 · Session 27 (11:34 AM CT, 16 Nov; started on time 17:34 UTC; no wait, no other run active)

Verification note frozen as v1.0, with a per-number account of what rests on one or two programs; Chen's theorem as a consistency test of the parity criterion (parity paper v0.12)

  • Verification note v1.0 (the bound share, priority 1 of the weekly review): a new section says for every number whether it rests on one program, two independent programs or a probabilistic check, and an appendix collects the facts about the capped problem (cap costs 0.0041 at $k=49,0$.00033 and 0.00054 at $k=48,0$.0095 and 0.0386 at $k=45$; ceilings 4.14 and 4.126). An internal referee pass on the candidate found 8 errors and 8 gaps, all fixed before the freeze. Among them: the 182 tail's second program was not written blind; the Bonferroni levels in the new appendix were reversed; and 'cannot reach 4.00438' for the Lemma 9.1 caps was too strong (now 'suggests').
  • Parity attempt (route card 2026-11-16-chen-consistency-test.md): the criterion of the parity paper does not block Chen's target 'p prime, p+2 in P_2': no non-negative weakly fair Liouville-pattern weight kills it (Lean-checked). It blocks each half: 1 - e1 e2 for twin primes, 1 + e1 e2 for p+2 exactly a product of two primes (Lean-checked). This matches Tao 2007 ('cannot hope to use plain sieve theory to ... just count semiprimes') and Pintz 2010 (p+2 with an odd number of prime factors infinitely often is not known). It is folklore in substance and entered the paper as one short remark.
  • Parity paper v0.12, cuts from the review: the Siegel-zero section went from 217 to about 95 lines, the Matomaki-Radziwill digest became one paragraph, the proofs of three propositions were shortened, and Prop. B-parity was reduced to its parity statement (now 29 pages). A fresh-context audit of every rewritten proof found no errors and 2 gaps (a dropped hypothesis, and 'under EH' where Bombieri's conclusion is also assumed), both fixed.
  • Main paper v0.27: three non-propositions demoted to remarks; Related results updated. Literature notes notes/sources-chen-parity.md. Chen-type constants: 1.104 Pi(x) (Wu 2004); 1.2759 (R. Li, preprint). Twin upper bound: 3.29956 Pi(x) (Lichtman). No improvement of Pintz's 16 was found in the citing papers.
  • Bounds unchanged: established 246; claimed 182; reproduced here 236 (conditional on Song-Yue Thm 4.18, not verified), 240 (conditional), 246 unconditional. The twin prime conjecture remains open.

Parity attempt Chen's theorem as a consistency test of the bipartite criterion: the class does not block 'p prime, p+2 in P_2' but blocks each half (Lean-checked weights). Classification: consistent (confirms the criterion; folklore); no parity-breaking content.

Explored Is there a 'mod q' analogue of the parity obstruction (weights in Omega(n) mod q)? Dead end, with a clean reason: fair Z/q weights killing $\mathrm{DHL}[k,m+1]$ exist iff k <= qm, so q = 3 would 'block' $\mathrm{DHL}[3,2]$, which is proved under GEH. The heuristic fails because sum z^Omega(n) ~ x (log x)^(z-1) G(z)/Gamma(z) has a non-zero main term unless 1/Gamma(z) = 0, which among |z| = 1 happens only at z = -1. At 1e7 the sums match the prediction to 3-9%, and a Selberg-type weight sees e(1/3)^Omega with modulus 0.44, against 0.06 for lambda.

2026-11-16 · Session 26 (3:34 AM CT, 16 Nov; started on time; no wait, no other run active)

Weekly review; Pintz's parity-sensitive dichotomy with Maynard weights: some even 0 < |d| <= 12 has lambda(p+d) = -1 for infinitely many primes (parity paper v0.11)

  • Weekly review (notes/reviews/2026-11-16.md, from a fresh-context candid review checked against the files). Top priorities for the week: freeze the verification note as v1.0, cut the parity paper to a ~20 pp. v1.0, and slim the main paper into an index. The $k=3$ GEH thread is stopped, rung 182 is paused until its data exist, and no capped-variational paper will start.
  • Parity attempt (route card 2026-11-16-pintz-dichotomy-maynard.md): Pintz's 2010 theorem was read in the source. It is a disjunction: two primes in n+H, or a prime n+h_i with lambda(n+r) = -1. Redone with Maynard's weights it needs only theta $M_{k}>1$ (bounded gaps need theta $M_{k}>2$), so 5-tuples suffice under Bombieri-Vinogradov, since $M_{5}\ge 1417255$/708216 > 2 (Maynard (8.17)). Consequence: some even d with 0 < |d| <= 12 has lambda(p+d) = -1 for infinitely many primes p (Pintz: 16); also 0 < d <= 14 (Pintz 18) and better conditional thresholds. Proved here, possibly folklore.
  • Computed: Maynard's $k=5$ ratio and optimised ratios for $k=2$..5 (thresholds 0.72154, 0.60752, 0.54230, 0.49898) agree exactly between two independent programs (code/parity/pintz_maynard.py, Dirichlet formula; pintz_maynard_check2.py, iterated sympy integration, written by a helper without seeing the first). A fresh-context adversarial audit found no errors and 11 wording or justification fixes, all applied (notes/audit-parity-v011.md).
  • The parity paper's criterion predicts this exactly: the target is the complete graph on H u {r}, which the class does not block; a single prescribed d is a star, which it does (Pintz's remark). So this sidesteps parity; it does not break it.
  • Papers: parity paper v0.11. New Section subsec:pintz; first review cuts (dual-formula subsection shortened, Prop. k3-step merged into Thm k3-window, $k=3$ remarks merged, Prop. tao-weight turned into a remark, answered open question and plan removed, changelog shortened, abstract rewritten, 174 words; a pre-existing citation error fixed). Verification note v0.12: wording of the Lemma 9.1 ceiling (one-program Gram certificate, probabilistic check of its weights), version tags removed. Main paper v0.26: version tags out of headings, Related results rewritten as current statements.
  • Bounds unchanged: established 246; claimed 182; reproduced here 236 (conditional on Song-Yue Thm 4.18, not verified), 240 (conditional), 246 unconditional. The twin prime conjecture remains open.

Flag for Jerry (minor) Pintz's 2010 parity-sensitive bound 16 improves to 12 with Maynard weights; proved here and checked, but probably folklore (literature question only).

Parity attempt Pintz's dichotomy with Maynard weights (two primes, or a prime next to lambda = sigma) needs only theta $M_{k}>1$: proved for 5-tuples under BV, |d| <= 12 instead of 16; classification: proved (modest, possibly folklore), sidesteps parity, does not break it.

Explored Can the dichotomy reach 4-tuples (|d| <= 8)? It needs level 0.5423 for primes and lambda, while the best known for smooth moduli is 0.525 (Stadlmann 2023), and enlarged supports need the lambda-twisted sum beyond moduli x^{1/2}: dead end with current inputs.

  • Murty-Vatwani still not obtainable (the Queen's URL returns the homepage HTML).

2026-11-16 · Session 25 (7:34 PM CT, 15 Nov; started on time 01:34 UTC 16 Nov; no wait)

The Siegel-zero world: where the parity obstruction's hypothesis can fail (parity paper v0.10, with a full referee pass)

  • Parity paper v0.10, thread 3 (hypotheses that imply twin primes): new Section on the Siegel-zero world, read in Tao-Teravainen arXiv:2109.06291v2. Heath-Brown's 1983 theorem is quoted from that paper, since the original is behind a Cloudflare challenge.
  • Prop. prop:siegel-bias (Proved here, elementary, possibly folklore): for a real character chi mod q and any +-1 completely multiplicative f, the class sums of f(n)f(n+2) mod Q = lcm(prod_{p<=w} p, q) are on average chi(b)chi(b+2) (x/Q)(1 - 4 $s_1$ - ...), where $s_1$ is the 1/p-sum of the primes where f and chi disagree. For q prime the discrepancies against the coprime average (as in Polymath8b (144)) are then large too, by the Jacobsthal sum -1.
  • Cor. cor:siegel-typeI: with a Siegel zero (TT (1.4), (3.22)-(3.23)), a per-modulus unweighted bound $\varepsilon$ x/Q on these discrepancies at Q = Wq forces the split primes of chi in (w, q^{1/log^{1-$\varepsilon$'} eta}) to have 1/p-sum >= (1-$\varepsilon$)/4 - o(1). So in a Siegel world with few small split primes, two-point Chowla holds (TT Cor. 1.8(ii)) but a per-modulus form of Chowla in progressions fails. That is where Heath-Brown's route escapes the obstruction. The averaged (EH-type) input is not shown to fail.
  • Checks: code/parity/siegel_class_bias.py verifies the pointwise inequality and part (2) exactly in 30 cases (q = 163, 1009; x up to 5e7). A fresh-context audit found 3 errors and 3 gaps (a misreading of TT (3.23), an overstated 'strictly stronger', a numerical sentence, error terms, per-modulus vs averaged, and the relation to open question 1), all fixed. A separate full-paper referee pass found no wrong proof; its framing and labelling fixes were applied (the H(2m+1) criterion is now stated as a barrier for proofs, not a bound; Bombieri's conditions are in the abstract; all 7 labels are defined; stale text is removed). The cuts it proposes are left to the weekly review.
  • Main paper v0.25 (Related results). Bounds unchanged: established 246, claimed 182, project 236 (conditional on Song-Yue's unverified Thm 4.18). The twin prime conjecture is open.

2026-11-15 · Session 24 (11:34 AM CT, started on time 17:35 UTC; no wait)

What the level of distribution alone gives for twin primes: Bombieri's asymptotic sieve read through Ford (parity paper v0.9); $k=45$ Lemma 9.1 weights checked by the second exact program

  • Parity paper v0.9 (thread 3, hypotheses that do not suffice): new Section on Bombieri's asymptotic sieve, read in Ford's 2004 paper (arXiv:math/0401215v1). Bombieri's theorem as Ford quotes it says that level of distribution x^nu for all nu < 1 (EH) gives the asymptotic for sum a_n Lambda_k(n), k >= 2, and Ford proves that no fixed nu < 1 suffices.
  • Prop. prop:bomb-twin (Proved here, folklore, conditional on Bombieri's theorem applying to a_n = Lambda(n+2)): under EH the twin-prime sum and the (Lambda*Lambda)-weighted count of p-2 = p1 p2 add up to 4 C_2 x log x, so twin primes are equivalent to that semiprime asymptotic, and EH gives the familiar factor-2 upper bound. New open question 7: Hooley's averaged parity condition for twins (a Moebius-on-shifted-primes statement) is open, and so is whether it would suffice.
  • Probable factor-2 slip found in Debouzy's 2019 preprint (twins almost prime under EH): the printed constant 2 S_2 should be 4 S_2. The p = 2 local factor is dropped in her eq. (65). Numerics (Heuristic): (P+Q)/(2M) = 0.968, 0.974, 0.975 at x = 1e6, 1e7, 3e7, tending to 1 for 4 S_2, against 1/2 for the printed constant. An adversarial audit confirmed it and also found a bug in our script, fixed before anything entered the paper.
  • Bound share: the $k=45$ Schwartz-Zippel check finished (capped_exact.py, 273 pieces, resumed from 74). Its exact I_T and J_T at the random test point equal capped_check.py's exactly (347- and 352-digit numerators), so the Lemma 9.1 ceiling < 3.99 now rests on two exact programs. Verification note v0.11, main paper v0.24.
  • Bounds unchanged: established 246, claimed 182, project 236 (conditional on Song-Yue's unverified Thm 4.18). The twin prime conjecture is open.

Flag for Jerry (minor) Debouzy arXiv:1907.06393v1: the main constant 2 S_2 appears to be off by a factor 2 (4 S_2 meant); qualitative result unaffected; confirmed by an independent audit and numerics (notes/flags-for-jerry.md).

Parity attempt Attempt: what Type I / level information alone gives for Lambda(n+2) (Bombieri, Ford). Classification: located (literature plus a folklore reduction). Under EH the barrier is exactly the prime / $P_2$ split of the Lambda_2 sum. First unresolved implication: whether EH plus Hooley's averaged absence of parity bias suffices (open per Ford), and whether that condition holds for mu(p-2) at all. No parity-breaking content.

2026-11-15 · Session 23 (back-to-back run 2 of 3 at Jerry's request, continued in the same conversation as Session 22 right after its merge, ~16:45 UTC; no wait)

For which $\varepsilon$ does Polymath8b's $k=3$ GEH criterion beat the parity value 2? Above 2 at $\varepsilon=2/9$, 1/4, 7/24; provably below 2 for every $\varepsilon\ge 0.439$ (parity paper v0.8)

  • Parity paper v0.8 (this run's parity attempt): for $\varepsilon\ge 1$/4 the $k=3$ problem of Polymath8b's Theorem 3.14 without the marginal condition reduces, by a Schur test, to a one-dimensional inequality (Prop. prop:unc-ceiling, proved here). Any positive phi satisfying it gives a ceiling for that $\varepsilon$ and every larger one.
  • Certificates, each checked by two independent programs (arb and mpmath interval arithmetic): the supremum sigma_3($\varepsilon$) is at most 1.9999 for every $\varepsilon$ in [0.439, 1/2), at most 1.995 for $\varepsilon\ge 0.45$, and at most 2.17 at $\varepsilon=1/4$. By scaling, the single certificate at 0.439 gives sigma_3($\varepsilon$) <= 3/2 + (4999/5610)(1-$\varepsilon$) on [1/4, 1/2), so sigma_3 < 2 for all $\varepsilon>0.43889$.
  • Lower side: exact step functions give sigma_3 > 2 at $\varepsilon=2/9$ and 7/24 as well as 1/4 (helper program plus our independent verifier). Exact lower bounds at 1/6, 1/5, 3/10, 1/3 stay below 2 and are undecided. So under GEH the criterion gives $\mathrm{DHL}[3,2]$ ($H_1$ <= 6) for 2/9, 1/4, 7/24 and cannot give it for $\varepsilon\ge 0.439$; heuristically it works only on about (0.19, 0.31), with maximum about 2.003.
  • Literature: Polymath8b v4 re-read (their partition works for $\varepsilon$ in [1/4, 1/3]; 1/4 chosen for simplicity, p. 63); three web searches found no range-of-$\varepsilon$ result. Fresh-context audit: no errors, 2 gaps and 6 wording items, all fixed (notes/audit-parity-v08.md). Main paper v0.23.
  • Bounds unchanged: established 246, claimed 182, project 236 (conditional on Song-Yue's unverified Thm 4.18). The twin prime conjecture is open.

Parity attempt Attempt: rigorous brackets for the $k=3$ GEH criterion as a function of $\varepsilon$. Classification: proved/computed (partial answer to open question 6): above 2 at 2/9, 1/4, 7/24, below 2 on [0.439, 1/2); first unresolved implication: a rigorous ceiling below 2 for small $\varepsilon$ or on [0.31, 0.4383), which must use the marginal condition. No parity-breaking content.

Explored Is the constant kappa of the 1D Schur problem (sup without marginal condition = 3/2 + kappa(1-$\varepsilon$)) a closed form? Dead end: kappa = 0.8901675 (threshold $\varepsilon$* = 0.43831) and mpmath.identify gives only overfitted noise; the scaling observation itself became Cor. cor:k3-linear.

2026-11-15 · Session 22 (back-to-back bonus run 1 of 3 at Jerry's request, started 9:51 AM CT / 15:51 UTC; no wait, no other run active)

Why the sharp $k=2$ argument does not extend to $k=3$: a dual formula, overlapping marginal conditions, and an exact step-function check of Polymath8b's $\mathrm{DHL}[3,2]$ under GEH (parity paper v0.7); our bipartite criterion credited to Tao's 2014 blog post

  • Parity paper v0.7 (this run's parity attempt): the supremum of Polymath8b's Theorem 3.14 ratio is the norm of a projected operator (Lemma lem:dual, standard duality). Our $k=2$ theorem is exactly this projection, because at $k=2$ the two marginal conditions act on disjoint sets. For k >= 3 they overlap (Prop. prop:overlap), so the argument does not transfer.
  • Dropping the overlapping conditions at $k=3$ lets the ratio exceed 2.1408 at $\varepsilon=1/8$ and 2.0657 at $\varepsilon=1/4$ (exact certificates, two scripts), while the true supremum at $\varepsilon=1/8$ is about 1.976 (Heuristic, two grid methods). So whether the criterion beats the parity value 2 is decided by the overlapping conditions.
  • An exact step function on the arrangement {t_i, t_i+t_j, sum t = m/8} gives the $k=3$, $\varepsilon=1/4$ ratio 2 + 711155/3866758832 (about 2.00018), confirmed by two independent programs (one written by a helper agent, one here). This independently checks the variational step of Polymath8b's Theorem 3.15 ($\mathrm{DHL}[3,2]$, so $H_1$ <= 6, under GEH); nothing new is proved. Heuristic $\varepsilon$ scan: only $\varepsilon=1/4$ of the sampled values exceeds 2 (about 2.003); 1/8, 3/8 give about 1.976 and 1.989.
  • Literature check correction: the core of our bipartite criterion (convex hull of forbidden sign patterns, bipartite target graphs, $\mathrm{DHL}[k,m+1]$ blocked iff k <= 2m) is already in Tao's blog post 'A general parity problem obstruction' (21 Nov 2014), found through a footnote of Akeno (arXiv:2508.02769v3). The paper now credits it (Remark rem:tao14b); our window form and H_m < H(2m+1) remain. Fresh-context audit of the new subsection: no mathematical errors, one numerical-description error and gaps, all fixed (notes/audit-parity-v07.md). Main paper v0.22.
  • Bound share: the 182 restoration step's per-component data (197 components) are not in the trial data we have; requested from Jerry (notes/sources-needed.md). Bounds unchanged: established 246, claimed 182, project 236 (conditional on Song-Yue's unverified Thm 4.18). The twin prime conjecture is open.

Parity attempt Attempt: extend the sharp $k=2$ variance argument to $k=3$ via a dual formula. Classification: no-go for the direct extension (Computed + Heuristic): the constraint cylinders overlap, and the clean-column relaxation is certified above 2.14 at $\varepsilon=1/8$ where the true value is about 1.976; first unresolved implication: a rigorous ceiling using the overlapping constraints. No parity-breaking content.

Explored Does the $k=3$ extremal keep the $k=2$ structure (constant marginal on the J-region)? Dead end: the grid extremal's marginal varies by a factor of about 5, and forcing a constant marginal drops the ratio to 1.736 (vs 1.959 on the same grid).

2026-11-15 · Session 21 (bonus run at Jerry's request, started 8:54 AM CT / 14:54 UTC; no wait, no other run active)

Where Polymath8b's GEH criteria stop: a ceiling k^2 log k/(k-1)^2 (no $\mathrm{DHL}[k,3]$ for k <= 45) and, at $k=2$, exactly the parity value 2 (parity paper v0.6); the 182 restoration arithmetic and source rows checked

  • Parity paper v0.6 (this run's parity attempt): Polymath8b's three GEH criteria (Thms 3.8, 3.12(ii), 3.14) have ratio at most c(k) = k^2 log k/(k-1)^2 (Prop. prop:ceil, proved here by scaling their Cor 6.4). So they cannot give $\mathrm{DHL}[k,3]$ for any k <= 45, $\mathrm{DHL}[5,3]$ in particular (c(5) = 2.515 < 4), although Liouville-pattern weights block only k <= 4. Thresholds 46, 392, 2965, 22007 for m = 2..5 are certified with ball arithmetic.
  • New Theorem thm:k2 (proved here, possibly folklore): at $k=2$ the Theorem 3.14 ratio is at most 2 for every $\varepsilon$ in (0,1), with equality for an explicit step function. So the strongest GEH criterion reaches exactly the parity value 2 and never exceeds it; with Cor. cor:consistent the criteria agree with the parity class on every blocked case. This confirms the obstruction; it does not break it.
  • Also in v0.6: the printed proof of Polymath8b's Prop 6.5 has an unjustified step (a weight can be -$\varepsilon$ off the fibres used); corrected constant $(\tfrac{k}{k-1}+\varepsilon)\log(2k-1)$; nothing in Polymath8b depends on it. Matomaki-Radziwill (arXiv:1501.04585v4) read in the source (thread 2). A fresh-context adversarial audit found no errors and 3 gaps (attribution to PM8b Remark 6.6, the m = 1 case of Cor. cor:consistent, a numerics reference), all fixed.
  • Bound share, rung 182: the printed arithmetic of the restoration step ((8.3), Sec. 10 totals, (10.2)-(10.3), Secs. 3.7, 13.3) was re-done exactly (86 checks pass), and the 43 + 55 source rows of the trial data satisfy every printed row definition (30 checks). The rows give T0 = 2491228441011/2624989000000 exactly. The 197 restoration integrals are not printed and remain unchecked. Verification note v0.10, main paper v0.21.
  • Bounds unchanged: established 246, claimed 182, project 236 (conditional on Song-Yue's unverified Thm 4.18). The twin prime conjecture is open.

Flag for Jerry Minor: one step of the printed proof of Polymath8b's Proposition 6.5 is not justified (corrected constant $(\tfrac{k}{k-1}+\varepsilon)\log(2k-1)$); no Polymath8b result depends on it.

Parity attempt Attempt: ceilings for Polymath8b's GEH criteria and the exact $k=2$ value. Classification: proved (confirms and locates the obstruction): for 5 <= k <= 45 the failure to give $\mathrm{DHL}[k,3]$ is a variational ceiling, not a parity block, and at $k=2$ the criterion sits exactly at 2. No parity-breaking content.

Explored How far above the parity value 2 is the Theorem 3.14 ratio at $k=3$ ($\varepsilon=1/4$)? Symmetric cube grids give 1.959 at n = 24 and extrapolate to about 2.003 (Heuristic), consistent with Polymath8b's certificate 2 + 5.8e-8; inconclusive (no certificate), recorded as open question 6.

2026-11-15 · Session 20 (bonus run at Jerry's request, started 5:37 AM CT / 11:37 UTC; no wait, no other run active)

What the integers lack compared with F_q[T]: Sawin-Shusterman's twin-prime proof read in the source and its split written over Z (parity paper v0.5); the 182 minorant's mass deficit by two methods

  • Parity paper v0.5 (thread 2 and this run's parity attempt): Sawin-Shusterman's proof of twin primes in F_q[T] (arXiv:1808.04001v2) read in the source. It has no sieve. It splits Lambda = -mu log * 1 at a level D, and handles the large-Mobius range through a characteristic-p identity (their Lemma 3.2: (r+s^p)' = r', so mu on a line is a quadratic character) together with character sums below the Burgess range.
  • Written over Z, the same split gives (Prop. prop:ss-split, proved here, elementary, possibly folklore; compare a related theorem of Murty-Vatwani, known to us only as quoted): unconditionally, by Bombieri-Vinogradov, sum Lambda(n)Lambda(n+2) ~ 2C_2 x holds if and only if T_D(x) = o(x), where T_D is a Mobius-prime correlation; under EH the same holds for any D = x^theta. This is a reformulation, not progress. Even the folklore conjecture on Mobius at shifted primes is weaker, by a factor log x, than what the e = 1 term of T_D needs.
  • Also in v0.5: Corollary cor:maynard-logc extends the twisted Type I input at level (log x)^{c/32} to Maynard's two-variable weights (conditional on the Tao-Teravainen preprint). Open question 3 is answered from the literature: no fixed-residue, absolute-value level beyond x^{1/2} for all moduli is known; the weighted records are 17/32 and 66/107; none reaches the 2/3 that the asymptotic sieve needs. A fresh-context adversarial audit found 2 errors, 3 gaps and 10 wording points, all fixed before release (notes/audit-parity-v05.md).
  • Bound share, rung 182: the mass deficit kappa_true of the five-prime minorant was recomputed by two independent methods: our own 3-variable reduction with Gauss-Legendre quadrature, and the paper's exact simplex moment series after checking that the simplices tile the region. They agree to 58 digits, kappa_true = 9.3307088324061199e-5. It lies inside the printed bracket and below kappa-bar and kappa_cvx. Verification note v0.9, main paper v0.20.
  • Bounds unchanged: established 246, claimed 182, project 236 (conditional on Song-Yue's unverified Thm 4.18). The twin prime conjecture is open.

Flag for Jerry Minor: Sawin-Shusterman's Theorem 4.5 states its saving exponent as beta/p, but its proof (p. 22) says beta/(2p). Most likely a harmless slip; the Annals version was not checked.

Parity attempt Attempt: locate the step of Sawin-Shusterman's twin-prime proof that has no integer analogue. Classification: located (reformulation). Over Z their split leaves T_D, a Mobius-prime correlation; the missing step is their characteristic-p Lemma 3.2. First unresolved implication: T_D(x) = o(x) for one admissible D.

Explored Can L^2 (Barban-Davenport-Halberstam) information on lambda(n)lambda(n+2) replace the pointwise twisted Type I input? Dead end: Cauchy-Schwarz caps any such route at level 1/2 even with random-sign variance, and the numerics at 10^7 look random (V(200)/(200N) = 0.983).

Did not work or is blocked

  • Wolfram connector not available in this session; mathbox not available (audits done by fresh-context helper agents). Murty-Vatwani (JNT 2017): the Queen's URL returned an HTML page, not the PDF; BFI (Acta 1986): bot challenge. Both are recorded in notes/sources-needed.md.

2026-11-15 · Session 19 follow-up (after Jerry uploaded Heath-Brown's paper, same day; no wait)

Heath-Brown's x^3+2y^3 read in the source: what its bilinear estimate uses (parity paper v0.4)

  • Jerry supplied the journal scan of Heath-Brown, Primes represented by x^3+2y^3 (Acta Math. 186 (2001)), requested in Session 19. Read in the source (theorem, outline, Lemmas 2.1, 3.8, 3.10, Sections 11-13) and added to the parity paper as Section subsec:hb-x3 (Literature) with Remark rem:hb-transfer (Heuristic).
  • The structure: Heath-Brown uses his own sieve close to Friedlander-Iwaniec's (their condition (R1) is not met); the Type I level X^{2-$\varepsilon$} comes from the factorisation of the form over Q(cbrt 2); in the Type II estimate the condition that alpha*beta_i has no cbrt4-coefficient makes the outer variable the cross product of the two inner ones, and the rest is a 3-dimensional large sieve plus Hecke Groessencharacters. Nothing like this exists for Lambda(n+2), where the same step leads back to a binary prime correlation.
  • The primary source corrects one point of the secondary notes (X. Li calls the sieve Harman's; Heath-Brown calls it his own version). Source request marked received. Bounds unchanged: established 246, claimed 182, project 236 (conditional). Twin prime conjecture open.

Did not work or is blocked

  • Wolfram and mathbox not available. Quotes checked against the page images by the run itself, not by a second reader.

2026-11-15 · Session 18 (3:34 AM CT slot, started early at Jerry's request, 10:23 PM CT 14 Nov; no wait, Session 17 had merged as PR #36)

An exact criterion for what the parity obstruction blocks (it explains the 6, and gives H_m < H(2m+1) for this class of weights); rung 182's numerators by a second program and its tail term recomputed

  • Parity paper v0.2 (papers/parity-barrier): for Polymath8b-type weights built from Liouville signs on the tuple, a weight that blocks a target exists exactly when (1/2,...,1/2) is a convex combination of target-free sets (Prop. prop:bipartite, proved here, possibly folklore). Consequences: a pair target is blocked iff its graph is bipartite; $\mathrm{DHL}[k,m+1]$ iff k <= 2m; and the class blocks every tuple exactly when g < H(2m+1), i.e. H_1 < 6, H_2 < 12, H_3 < 20, while the known sieve bounds for H_2 are 270 (EH) and 252 (GEH). Finite cases Lean-checked (lean/TwinPrimes/Parity.lean); H(3..9) = 6, 8, 12, 16, 20, 26, 30 by exhaustive search.
  • The weight printed in Polymath8b's Remark 8.1 vanishes identically (the product includes i = i', and even with i < i' the positions prime to 3 contain triangles); the intended weight (odd positions) is fair and works. Typo-level; flagged as a minor item.
  • Thread 2: Friedlander-Iwaniec's a^2+b^4 paper read in the source (Theorem 1, Props. 3.5, 4.1, 17.2; norm-form factorisation, lattice count, spin symbol) and a remark on why that structure has no counterpart for Lambda(n+2). Quantitative two-point Chowla literature read for open question 1 (Tao-Teravainen preprint would give level (log x)^c0; deduction not written). A fresh-context adversarial audit found 2 errors (an overclaimed scope in Cor. cor:six, one page number) and several gaps; all fixed before release.
  • Rung 182 (bound share): a second program for the cap numerators gives J0 = 5.63645908992367e-11 and J+ = 4.08240742966434e-13 as rigorous balls, both above the published lower bounds and agreeing with program 1 to 2e-23 / 2e-32. The tail term, transcribed from the bundle (kernel K of (5.6) computed exactly), is T = 5.286348805928e-13, just below its published upper bound, so q_H - 1 = 2.767059e-4 >= the certified mu- (one program for the tail). 182 stays claimed: restoration losses and analytic inputs are unchecked.
  • Small results (3g): the H_m programme was scoped (notes/hm-programme.md): Polymath8b's Theorem 6.7 lower bound reproduced in ball arithmetic ($k=35410$ with MPZ, 41588 with BV only, both as published); any gain from the 2026 inputs depends on a (varpi, delta) region no source states, so no paper yet. Bounds unchanged: established 246, claimed 182, project 236 (conditional). Main paper v0.18; parity paper v0.2.

Flag for Jerry Minor: Polymath8b's Remark 8.1 prints a parity weight that is identically zero (typo-level; the intended weight works); the journal version could not be checked.

Parity attempt Which close-prime targets the Polymath8b obstruction blocks: proved (for the class of Liouville-pattern weights) that it is exactly the bipartite / fractional-stable-set condition, which explains the parity limit 6 and gives H_m < H(2m+1) for this class; the first open step is whether weights outside the class block $\mathrm{DHL}[5,3]$.

2026-11-12 · Session 6 (12:39 PM CT, started by hand in place of the 3:34 PM slot)

Variational step of Stadlmann's 240 reproduced exactly: capped ratio 4.0120 > 4 (two independent programs); project bound 240, conditional on his unverified equidistribution estimates; paper v0.6

  • Proved an exact reduction of Stadlmann's capped functionals (Prop. 25): the fibre over u' is [0, min(R, max(g, C_{m+1} - s))], and I_T, J_T are the cap-free values minus three corrections, each a signed inclusion-exclusion sum (m <= 9 large coordinates, j <= 3 extra) of polygon integrals of polynomials in two variables. 2026-11-12 · Session 4 (8 AM, first test run of the schedule)

$H_1\le 246$ reproduced ($M_{50,1/25}\ge 4.0043063$); dual weights that see only $t_m$ and S stall near 4.14; paper v0.4

  • Added Polymath8b's $\varepsilon$-enlarged simplex to the exact pipeline (scaling plus the binomial rewrite $1+\varepsilon-P_1=2\varepsilon+(1-\varepsilon-P_1)$); checked every Gram entry against direct sympy integration for $k=3,4$. Lowering the search precision from about 6000 to 300-500 bits made high degrees cheap.
  • Certified $M_{50,1/25}\ge 4.0012466$ (degree 25, 1780 coefficients) and $\ge 4.0043063$ (degree 27, 2526 coefficients), matching Polymath8b's 4.00124 and 4.0043. With the Lean tuple (50, 246) the project's end-to-end bound moved from 270 to 246, the established bound: a reproduction, not new.
  • Own idea (variational/spectral): optimised Lemma 13 over every dual weight of the form $\Psi(S-t_m,t_m)$ as a convex program. It returns exactly $\tfrac{k}{k-1}\log k$ at $\varepsilon=0$ and settles near 4.14 at (50, 1/25), far above the truth ($\ge 4.0043$): sharper floors need weights that see more of t (Proposition 19). A Collatz-Wielandt weight from the Galerkin eigenfunction is within 1e-4 of $M_{3}$ at $k=3$ (sampled), but for $\varepsilon>0$ the eigenfunction vanishes where no index is active.
  • Certified $M_{k,\varepsilon}$ for $k=49,50$ at degrees 10-24 and six values of $\varepsilon$. A geometric extrapolation in the degree, tested against the known $k=50$ values and against a prediction registered before the degree-24 run (3.98230 predicted, 3.98218 certified), suggests $\sup_\varepsilon M_{49,\varepsilon}\approx 3.991$–$3.994<4$: heuristic evidence that the enlarged simplex stops at $k=50$ (Remark 20).
  • Lean: not_boundedGaps_one (gaps of size 1 happen only once) and admissible_pair_iff ({0,d} is admissible iff d is even). Re-read Polymath8b Prop. 6.5 in the arXiv text: as printed, the argument gives $(\tfrac{k}{k-1}+\varepsilon)\log(2k-1)$ (Remark 16).

Did not work or is blocked

  • The first discretisations of the dual-weight program were wrong or unstable (Kelley cutting planes drifted; piecewise-constant weights in (sigma,u) could not represent the two-zone weight). The final version uses cells in (S,u) and an exact convex solver per step.
  • At $(k,\varepsilon)=(30,1/4)$ the discretised optimum has not converged (4.050, 4.035, 4.024 for N = 80, 120, 160, above the 4.0021 already certified), so the exact floor of the $(t_m,S)$ family is not determined.
  • A product-form Collatz-Wielandt weight ($F=\prod_i 1/(1+At_i)$) gave useless bounds (above 5 at $k=50$).
  • No news search this run (only the 3:34 AM run does it); the 182 preprint is still not re-opened.

Proved hereParity paper v0.13, Prop. prop:gowers-blind (folklore in substance): with Tao's local norm ||f||_{U^d[N]}, for d >= 1, L >= 2, N >= 4d there are f_+-: [N] -> {-1,1} with ||f||_{U^d[N]}^{2^d} <= 2^{2d+1}(4d)^d L/N and +-(1/N) sum f(n)f(n+2) >= 1 - 4/L - 6/N, and a {0,2}-valued g with ||g-1||_{U^d}^{2^d} <= 3 c_d L/N, mean within (3L/N)^{1/2} of 1, and (1/N) sum g(n)g(n+2) <= 4/L + 2(3L/N)^{1/2} + 8/N (random block functions). So global Gowers uniformity (all that the Green-Tao finite-complexity theory uses) says nothing about two-point Chowla or twin primes; Tao's localisation (arXiv:1605.04628) sidesteps this for lambda only.papers/parity-barrier (subsec:gowers); code/parity/gowers_twin_blocks.py; notes/parity-attempts/2026-11-17-gowers-infinite-complexity.md; notes/audit-parity-v013.md

Proved here(possibly folklore) Schur-test reduction: for $k=3$ and 1/4 <= $\varepsilon<1$/2, any positive phi on [0,1-$\varepsilon$] with int_0^{a-y}(phi(t)+phi(y))/(C-3/2+t+y)dt <= phi(y) proves that the Theorem 3.14 ratio (even without the vanishing marginal condition) is <= C for all $\varepsilon$' >= $\varepsilon$; the condition is scale-invariant.papers/parity-barrier v0.8 Prop. prop:unc-ceiling, Cor. cor:k3-linear
ComputedFor which $\varepsilon$ Polymath8b's $k=3$ GEH criterion works: the supremum sigma_3($\varepsilon$) exceeds 2 at $\varepsilon=2/9$, 1/4, 7/24 (exact step functions), and is <= 1.9999 < 2 for every $\varepsilon$ in [0.439, 1/2) (Schur certificates); sigma_3(1/4) in (2.0001, 2.17], and sigma_3($\varepsilon$) <= 3/2 + 0.89109(1-$\varepsilon$) on [1/4, 1/2). Each certificate by two independent programs. Heuristic: the good set is about (0.19, 0.31) with maximum about 2.003.papers/parity-barrier v0.8 Thm. thm:k3-window; code/parity/k3_unc_cert.py, k3_unc_verify.py, k3_arrangement_exact.py, k3_arrangement_verify.py
Proved herePintz's dichotomy with Maynard weights: for an admissible k-tuple H, r not in H, sigma = +-1, if theta $M_{k}>1$ (theta a level for primes and for lambda; theta = 1/2 by BV) then infinitely many n have two primes in n+H or a prime n+h_i with lambda(n+r) = sigma. Hence ($k=5$, $M_{5}\ge 1417255$/708216) some even 0 < |d| <= 12 (and some even 0 < d <= 14) has lambda(p+d) = -1 for infinitely many primes p (Pintz 2010: 16 and 18). Possibly folklore (three web searches found no improvement of Pintz). Ratios exact by two programs. The target is the complete graph on H u {r} (not blocked by the parity class); a single prescribed d is a star (blocked).papers/parity-barrier v0.11 Thm. thm:pintz-maynard, Cor. cor:pintz12, Rem. rem:pintz-parity; code/parity/pintz_maynard.py, pintz_maynard_check2.py

The paper

Toward the twin prime conjecture: certificates, limits of the sieve, and a working programme

Jerry Kim

Version 0.29, 17 November 2026

This is a living working paper. It does not prove the twin prime conjecture, and it does not improve the best known bound on small gaps between primes. It records three things. First, the state of the problem, keeping three numbers apart: the established bound H_1\le246 (Polymath8b), the smallest claimed bound 182 (2026 preprints), and what this project reproduces for itself: 246 unconditionally, and 240 and 236 conditionally on analytic inputs we have not verified. Second, what we have checked independently: the combinatorial half in Lean 4, and the variational step of the 2026 claims 240, 236, 212 and 186 in exact or ball arithmetic, each by two independent programs. Third, small results of our own: a rigidity theorem for the pigeonhole step, floors and ceilings for the \varepsilon-enlarged and capped variational problems, exact formulas for capped functionals, and one-sided certificates. Every statement carries a label (Lean-checked, Computed, Proved here, Literature, Claimed, Open, Heuristic); the history of the document is in Appendix [app:history].

The problem and the honest position

Write p_n for the n-th prime and H_1=\liminf_{n\to\infty}(p_{n+1}-p_n). The twin prime conjecture is the statement H_1=2. It is Open.

A finite set \mathcal H=\{h_1<\dots<h_k\} of integers is admissible if for every prime p it misses at least one residue class modulo p. Let \mathrm{DHL}[k,2] be the statement that every admissible k-tuple has infinitely many translates n+\mathcal H containing at least two primes, and let H(k) be the least diameter h_k-h_1 of an admissible k-tuple. Then

\mathrm{DHL}[k,2]\ \Longrightarrow\ H_1\le H(k),\qquad(1)
(Literature; Lean-checked for the pairs of Theorem 1), and \mathrm{DHL}[2,2] applied to \{0,2\} is the twin prime conjecture. All progress on H_1 since 2013 consists of proving \mathrm{DHL}[k,2] for smaller k.

Where the bound stands

H_1\le k Source Label
7\times10^7 3.5\times10^6 Zhang [21] Literature
600 105 Maynard [10] Literature
246 50 Polymath8b [15] Literature
240 49^{\ast} Stadlmann [19] Claimed^{\dagger}
236 48 Kintali [8]; Song–Yue [18] Claimed^{\ddagger}
212 45 Charton et al. [3] Claimed
186 40 OpenAI [13] Claimed^{\S}
182 39 GPT-6 Astra, prompted by Sharifi [16] Claimed
12 5 Maynard, assuming EH [10] Literature
6 3 Polymath8b, assuming GEH [15] Literature

The bound 246 stood from 2014 until the last days of August 2026. It has been formalised in Lean 4, assuming only the Bombieri–Vinogradov theorem [1]. The bounds from 240 to 186 appeared within one week. They rest on Stadlmann’s idea of enlarging the support of the sieve weights using equidistribution estimates for moduli with a large smooth factor.

  • 186. The Lean development accompanying it is conditional on three stated inputs (two exponential-sum bounds and a family of numerical integral bounds) [13]. An independent re-run of that repository, dated 7 and 8 November 2026, reproduced the build and the numerical certificate and describes the result as “conditionally supported, with the computation independently reproduced; not yet settled”; the two exponential-sum bounds remain axioms [12].

  • 182. A further document, dated 18 September 2026 and naming an AI system as its author, claims \mathrm{DHL}[39,2] and hence 182; by its own abstract its Lean development verifies the last implication “under explicit numerical and finite-field premises” [16]. We had missed it in earlier versions. (These details were read by an earlier run of this project; on 12 November the file was too large for our reader and direct download was refused, so we have not re-checked them since.)

  • Status elsewhere. As of 7 September 2026 a standard reference still listed 246 as the best fully established value [11], and the page read the same on 12 November. On the date of this version we found no report of complete independent verification of any of the 2026 claims and no claimed bound below 182.

We therefore keep three numbers apart throughout:

  • established: 246;

  • claimed: 182;

  • reproduced by this project: 246 unconditionally; 240, conditionally on the reduction [19, Prop. 1] and the equidistribution estimates of [19], which we have not verified (Corollary 29); and 236, conditionally on the equidistribution theorem of [18] and their reduction [18, Thm. 4.33, §§4.12–4.14] (Corollary 39), which we have not verified either.

Why 2 is a different problem

Under the generalised Elliott–Halberstam conjecture the sieve gives H_1\le6, and Polymath8b showed that 6 cannot be improved by purely sieve-theoretic arguments: this is Selberg’s parity obstruction [15]. Lowering the bound (Track A below) and proving the conjecture (Track B) are therefore different tasks. No amount of success on Track A reaches 2.

What this project has verified for itself

The combinatorial half, checked by the Lean kernel

Our Lean development is written in core Lean 4, without Mathlib. It defines primality, the twin prime conjecture, the predicate BoundedGaps B (infinitely many pairs of distinct primes at distance at most B), admissibility, and \mathrm{DHL}[k,2]. It contains no sorry, and the only axioms it uses are propext and Quot.sound. The statement \mathrm{DHL}[k,2] is always a hypothesis, never an axiom.

Theorem 1 (Lean-checked).

  1. The twin prime conjecture is equivalent to BoundedGaps 2.

  2. If a finite Boolean check succeeds on a list \mathcal H (it misses a class modulo every m with 2\le m\le|\mathcal H|), then \mathcal H is admissible. The moduli p>|\mathcal H| are handled by a formal pigeonhole argument.

  3. For each pair (k,D) in Table 1, for (k,D)=(56,278), (57,282), (58,288), (59,300), (60,304), (61,310), (62,320), (63,324), (64,330), and for (102,578) and (105,600), there is an explicit admissible k-tuple in [0,D], and \mathrm{DHL}[k,2] implies BoundedGaps D. In particular \mathrm{DHL}[2,2] implies the twin prime conjecture.

  4. \mathrm{DHL}[k,2] implies \mathrm{DHL}[k',2] for every k'\ge k (DHL.mono: delete shifts from the tuple). So a proof of \mathrm{DHL}[j,2] with j\le k gives BoundedGaps D for each listed pair (k,D).

  5. BoundedGaps 1 is false (not_boundedGaps_one), and a pair \{0,d\} is admissible if and only if d is even (admissible_pair_iff). So 2 is the smallest bound of this form that can hold, and the twin prime conjecture is the strongest statement of the form BoundedGaps D.

Pairs (k,D) with a kernel-checked admissible k-tuple of diameter D. The tuples were found by our own search; each D agrees with the value of H(k) in published tables, whose optimality we have not re-proved.
k D k D k D k D k D k D
2 2 11 36 20 80 29 130 38 176 47 226
3 6 12 42 21 84 30 136 39 182 48 236
4 8 13 48 22 90 31 140 40 186 49 240
5 12 14 50 23 94 32 146 41 188 50 246
6 16 15 56 24 100 33 152 42 196 51 252
7 20 16 60 25 110 34 156 43 200 52 254
8 26 17 66 26 114 35 158 44 210 53 264
9 30 18 70 27 120 36 162 45 212 54 270
10 32 19 76 28 126 37 168 46 216 55 272

Theorem 1 is elementary. Its value is that implication (1) is now a checked interface: any future proof of \mathrm{DHL}[k,2], by anyone, yields the corresponding gap bound with nothing left to trust on the combinatorial side.

The sieve constant, with exact certificates

Let \mathcal{R}_k=\{t\in[0,1]^k:\ t_1+\dots+t_k\le1\} and, for square-integrable F supported on \mathcal{R}_k,

I_k(F)=\int_{\mathcal{R}_k}F^2,\qquad J_k^{(m)}(F)=\int\left(\int F\,dt_m\right)^{2}\prod_{i\ne m}dt_i,\qquad M_k=\sup_F\frac{\sum_{m=1}^{k}J_k^{(m)}(F)}{I_k(F)}.
Maynard’s theorem [10, Prop. 4.2] states that if the primes have level of distribution \theta and \theta M_k/2>1 then \mathrm{DHL}[k,2] holds. With Bombieri–Vinogradov (\theta<1/2) the condition is M_k>4.

We take F=\sum_{a+2b\le d}c_{a,b}(1-P_1)^aP_2^b with P_j=\sum_it_i^j, as in [10, §8]. All integrals reduce to

\int_{\mathcal{R}_k}(1-P_1)^AP_2^B\,dt=\frac{A!}{(k+A+2B)!}\,G_k(B),\qquad \sum_{B\ge0}G_k(B)\frac{x^B}{B!}=\left(\sum_{b\ge0}\frac{(2b)!}{b!}x^b\right)^{k},
which follows from the Dirichlet integral. The two quadratic forms are then exact rational matrices. We compute an approximate top eigenvector in floating point, round it to rationals, and evaluate the Rayleigh quotient in exact arithmetic; the last step is a rigorous lower bound whatever happened before it.

Theorem 2 (Computed, exact rational arithmetic). With F a polynomial in 1-P_1 and P_2 only,

M_{105}\ge4.00206976,\qquad M_{102}\ge4.00271343,\qquad M_{5}\ge2.00410541 .

The first value reproduces Maynard’s M_{105}>4 with d=11 (42 coefficients). The third gives \mathrm{DHL}[5,2] and H_1\le12 under the Elliott–Halberstam conjecture, again reproducing [10]. For d=11,15,19 the least k at which this family certifies M_k>4 is 105,102,102, and at k=101 it gives only 3.99931 (d=19): polynomials in P_1,P_2 alone stop at k=102.

All symmetric test functions

To go further we allow every symmetric polynomial. Since P_1,\dots,P_k generate the symmetric polynomials, the functions

(1-P_1)^a\,P^{b}:=(1-P_1)^a\prod_{j\ge2}P_j^{b_j},\qquad a+w(b)\le d,\quad w(b)=\sum_j jb_j,
span all symmetric polynomials of degree at most d (for k\ge d). The integrals have a closed form that we have not seen stated in this way.

Lemma 3 (Proved here; possibly folklore). Let X_1,\dots,X_k be independent exponential random variables of mean 1 and E_k(b)=\mathbb E\prod_jP_j(X)^{b_j}. Then

\int_{\mathcal{R}_k}(1-P_1)^AP^{b}\,dt=\frac{A!}{(k+A+w(b))!}\,E_k(b), \qquad \sum_b E_k(b)\frac{s^b}{b!}=\left(\sum_{c}w(c)!\,\frac{s^c}{c!}\right)^{k},
where s^b=\prod_js_j^{b_j} and b!=\prod_jb_j!. In particular each E_k(b) is a positive integer, computed exactly by multiplying exponential generating functions.

Proof. For a monomial, the Dirichlet integral gives \int_{\mathcal{R}_k}(1-P_1)^At^\alpha\,dt=A!\,\alpha!/(k+A+|\alpha|)!, and \alpha!=\mathbb EX^\alpha. Summing over the monomials of P^b, all of degree w(b), gives the first identity. For the second, \mathbb E\exp(\sum_js_jP_j(X))=\left(\mathbb E\exp(\sum_js_jX_1^j)\right)^k by independence, and \mathbb E\exp(\sum_js_jX_1^j)=\sum_c\mathbb E X_1^{w(c)}\,s^c/c!=\sum_cw(c)!\,s^c/c!, as formal power series. ◻

The J-functional reduces to the same lemma in k-1 variables: with P_j=P_j'+t_k^j,

\int_0^{1-P_1'}(1-P_1)^aP^b\,dt_k=\sum_{e\le b}\binom{b}{e} \frac{a!\,w(e)!}{(a+w(e)+1)!}\,(1-P_1')^{a+w(e)+1}P'^{\,b-e}.
So both Gram matrices are exact rational matrices. We find an approximate top eigenvector by inverse iteration with a fixed shift above \frac{k}{k-1}\log k, which can only converge to the top eigenvalue. We take its floating-point midpoints as exact dyadic rationals and evaluate the Rayleigh quotient exactly. The working precision of the eigenvector matters only for speed. A separate script, code/verify_cert.py, rebuilds both Gram matrices from scratch and re-evaluates each stored coefficient vector in exact arithmetic. (No coefficient vector is stored in the repository for the value M_{105}\ge4.17427 below, so that value is not covered by this re-check.)

Theorem 4 (Computed, exact rational arithmetic).

\begin{gathered} M_{105}\ge4.17427,\qquad M_{64}\ge4.00363,\qquad M_{58}\ge4.00927,\\ M_{56}\ge4.00519,\qquad M_{55}\ge4.00516,\qquad M_{54}\ge4.00238 . \end{gathered}
The first two use all symmetric polynomials of degree 11 and 12 (195 and 272 coefficients). The others use the subspace spanned by (1-P_1)^aP^b with b supported on even j, as in [15, §7], of degree 16, 18, 20 and 23 (307, 471, 707 and 1236 coefficients).

Basis degree coefficients least k with certified M_k>4
1-P_1, P_2 19 110 102
all symmetric 12 272 64
even power sums 16 307 58
even power sums 18 471 56
even power sums 20 707 55^{\dagger}
even power sums 23 1236 54^{\dagger}

Corollary 5 (Computed and Lean-checked, conditional on Literature inputs; end-to-end within this project). Assuming the Bombieri–Vinogradov theorem and [10, Prop. 4.2], both Literature, Theorems 4 and 1 give H_1\le270.

This reproduces a known result and is not new. Polymath8b obtained M_{54}>4.00238 with even-part polynomials of degree 23 [15, Thm. 3.9], and hence H_1\le270. Our certified value in the same space, 4.002386\ldots, agrees with theirs to every digit they print, which is an external check on our pipeline; two independent runs of it (one from an earlier session) produced the same value. Corollary 5 is the best bound certified on the plain simplex with Bombieri–Vinogradov, by Polymath8b and by us; the upper bound of the next subsection excludes only k\le50 there, so k=51,52,53 (H(k)=252,254,264) are not ruled out by anything in this paper. Corollary 7 below takes the next rung of the ladder 600\to270\to246\to240\to212\to186\to182.

What the plain method cannot do

Polymath8b proved (Literature) that M_k\le\frac{k}{k-1}\log k [15]. Hence (Computed from that bound) M_k>4 forces k\ge51, and on the plain simplex \mathrm{DHL}[k,2] requires a level of distribution \theta>2(k-1)/(k\log k) (values truncated):

k 50 45 40 35 30 20 10 5
\theta> 0.501 0.513 0.528 0.546 0.568 0.634 0.781 0.994

The 2026 results go below k=50 by changing the support of F, not by proving a uniform level of distribution of this size.

Beyond the plain simplex: H_1\le246

Polymath8b’s \varepsilon-enlarged simplex [15, Thm. 3.12] (Literature; we re-read the statement in the arXiv text for this version) takes F supported on (1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{R}_k and integrates the square of each marginal \int F\,dt_m only over (1-\varepsilon)\mathcal{R}_{k-1}. Call the resulting constant M_{k,\varepsilon}. If \mathrm{EH}[\theta] holds with 1+\varepsilon<1/\theta and M_{k,\varepsilon}>2/\theta, then \mathrm{DHL}[k,2] holds. Bombieri–Vinogradov gives every \theta<1/2, so for every fixed \varepsilon<1 the condition is M_{k,\varepsilon}>4.

We extended our pipeline to this functional, following the route that [15, §7.2] describes in words. Put L=1+\varepsilon, \tau=1-\varepsilon and use the basis (L-P_1)^aP^b (b supported on even j). The following identities are elementary (Proved here): the substitution t=Ls and Lemma 3 give

\int_{L\mathcal{R}_k}(L-P_1)^AP^b\,dt=L^{k+A+w(b)}\,\frac{A!}{(k+A+w(b))!}\,E_k(b);
the inner integral over t_m\in[0,L-P_1'] is the formula displayed after Lemma 3 with 1-P_1' replaced by L-P_1'; and on \tau\mathcal{R}_{k-1} we write L-P_1'=2\varepsilon+(\tau-P_1'), so that
\int_{\tau\mathcal{R}_{k-1}}(L-P_1')^AP'^{\,B}=\sum_{i=0}^{A}\binom Ai(2\varepsilon)^{A-i}\, \tau^{k-1+i+w(B)}\,\frac{i!}{(k-1+i+w(B))!}\,E_{k-1}(B).
For rational \varepsilon both Gram matrices are again exact rational matrices. We checked the code against hand computation for k=2,3 and F\in\{1,L-P_1\}, and against direct iterated symbolic integration (sympy, without Lemma 3 or the binomial rewrite) of every Gram entry for (k,d,\varepsilon)=(3,3,1/5) and (4,4,1/25): no mismatch (code/check_eps_sympy.py). At \varepsilon=0 it is the old code path (an old certificate re-verifies unchanged). We also found that the working precision of the eigenvector search can be cut from about 6000 to 300–400 bits without changing any certified digit, which makes degree 25 feasible on a four-core machine (build 16 minutes, search 4 minutes).

Theorem 6 (Computed, exact rational arithmetic). With even power sums of degree 25 (1780 coefficients) and of degree 27 (2526 coefficients),

M_{50,1/25}\ge4.0012465\qquad\text{and}\qquad M_{50,1/25}\ge4.0043062 .

The certificates are code/cert_M50e25_D25e.json and code/cert_M50e25_D27e.json (the degree-27 run took 43 minutes to build and 10 minutes to search, with about 10 GB of memory); the script code/verify_cert.py rebuilds both Gram matrices from scratch and re-evaluates the stored rational vector exactly; it uses the same Gram-matrix builder, so it checks the arithmetic and the bookkeeping, while the formulas themselves are checked by the symbolic test above. Polymath8b report M_{50,1/25}>4.00124 at degree 25 and >4.0043 at degree 27 [15, §7.2]; our values agree with both to every printed digit.

Corollary 7 (Computed and Lean-checked, conditional on Literature inputs; end-to-end within this project). Assuming the Bombieri–Vinogradov theorem and [15, Thm. 3.12(i)], both Literature, Theorem 6 and the Lean tuple (50,246) of Theorem 1 give H_1\le246.

This is the established bound of 2014, reproduced; it is not new. It closes the second rung of our ladder. Table 2 shows how the certified values grow with the degree and depend on \varepsilon; it is the data behind Remark 22 below.

Results of our own: rigidity of the pigeonhole step

Every bound above ends with the same step: if the weighted average number of primes among n+h_1,\dots,n+h_k exceeds 1, two of them are prime, and they are at most h_k-h_1 apart. A natural hope is to do better with unequal weights. Fix D, and give each position a weight c_i\ge0. Call a set of positions D-separated if any two of its shifts differ by more than D, and let \alpha_D(c) be the largest total weight of a D-separated set. If no two primes among the n+h_i are within D, then \sum_ic_i\mathbf 1_{n+h_i\text{ prime}}\le\alpha_D(c). Writing

M_k[c]=\sup_F\frac{\sum_{m}c_mJ_k^{(m)}(F)}{I_k(F)},
the sieve therefore yields H_1\le D as soon as \tfrac{\theta}{2}M_k[c]>\alpha_D(c). For c=(1,\dots,1) and D=h_k-h_1 this is the usual criterion. Could a cleverer c certify some D<h_k-h_1 that no sub-tuple certifies?

Proposition 8 (Proved here; possibly folklore). Let \omega be the largest number of shifts h_i contained in an interval of length D. Then for every c\ge0, not identically zero,

\frac{M_k[c]}{\alpha_D(c)}\le M_\omega .
So the weighted criterion never certifies more than the standard criterion applied to the best sub-tuple of diameter at most D.

Proof. Let G be the graph on \{1,\dots,k\} joining i\ne j when |h_i-h_j|\le D. Its stable sets are the D-separated sets and its cliques are the sets of shifts lying in an interval of length D. Normalise \alpha_D(c)=1, so c lies in

P=\{c\ge0:\ c(S)\le1\text{ for every stable set }S\}.
The graph G is a unit interval graph, hence perfect, and so is its complement by the perfect graph theorem [9]. For a perfect graph the polytope cut out by the clique inequalities is the convex hull of the indicator vectors of its stable sets [5]. Applying this to the complement of G gives
P=\operatorname{conv}\{\mathbf 1_K:\ K\text{ a clique of }G\}.

The map c\mapsto M_k[c] is a supremum of linear functionals, hence convex, and therefore M_k[c]\le\max_KM_k[\mathbf 1_K]. It remains to show M_k[\mathbf 1_K]\le M_{j} for |K|=j; this suffices because M_j is non-decreasing in j (adjoin a variable and concentrate F near its zero set). Write t=(s,u) with s the coordinates in K and u the others, put \sigma(u)=1-\sum u\ge0, and let F_u=F(\cdot,u), a function on the scaled simplex \sigma(u)\mathcal{R}_j. For m\in K we have J_k^{(m)}(F)=\int J_j^{(m)}(F_u)\,du, and I_k(F)=\int I_j(F_u)\,du. Rescaling F_u to \mathcal{R}_j multiplies I_j by \sigma^{j} and each J_j^{(m)} by \sigma^{j+1}, so

\sum_{m\in K}J_j^{(m)}(F_u)\ \le\ \sigma(u)\,M_j\,I_j(F_u)\ \le\ M_j\,I_j(F_u).
Integrating over u gives the claim. ◻

Remark 9 (Computed, consistency check for k=3). Take the shifts (0,1,2) and D=1, so that \omega=2 and \alpha_D(c)=1 for both weight vectors below. With unrestricted polynomials of degree 6 on \mathcal{R}_3 we find the lower bounds M_3[(1,1,1)]\ge1.6464, M_3[(1,1,0)]\ge1.3579 and M_3[(\tfrac12,1,\tfrac12)]\ge1.1604, against M_2=1/(1-W(1/e))=1.3859\ldots . The half-weighted functional is far below M_2, as the proposition requires.

The same rigidity on the enlarged simplex

The \varepsilon-enlarged simplex of Polymath8b [15, Thm. 3.12] takes F supported on (1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{R}_k and restricts the outer integral of each J^{(m)} to (1-\varepsilon)\mathcal{R}_{k-1}. Call the resulting constant M_{k,\varepsilon}. The criterion M_{k,\varepsilon}>2/\theta is valid in two cases (Literature): under \mathrm{EH}[\theta] when 1+\varepsilon<1/\theta, and under \mathrm{GEH}[\theta] when \varepsilon<1/(k-1). With Bombieri–Vinogradov the first case allows every \varepsilon<1, and Polymath8b’s H_1\le246 uses M_{50,1/25}>4.0043 [15, Thm. 3.13]. Unequal weights are no more useful there.

Proposition 10 (Proved here; possibly folklore). For 0\le\varepsilon<1 and every weight c\ge0, c\ne0, M_{k,\varepsilon}[c]/\alpha_D(c)\le M_{\omega,\varepsilon}, with equality for the indicator of a largest clique. Since the conditions on \varepsilon above do not get harder as k decreases, the weighted criterion never certifies more than the standard one for the best sub-tuple of diameter at most D.

Proof. As for Proposition 8, it suffices to show \sum_{m\in K}J_{k,\varepsilon}^{(m)}(F)\le M_{j,\varepsilon}I_k(F) for a clique K of size j, together with M_{j,\varepsilon}\le M_{\omega,\varepsilon}. Fix the coordinates u outside K, with |u|\le1-\varepsilon (other slices do not meet the J-region). The slice F_u is supported on (1+\varepsilon-|u|)\mathcal{R}_j, and its J-region is (1-\varepsilon-|u|)\mathcal{R}_{j-1}. Put \mu=(1+\varepsilon-|u|)/(1+\varepsilon)\le1 and H(x)=F_u(\mu x), which is supported on (1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{R}_j. Then I_j(F_u)=\mu^jI_j(H), and each slice J-term equals \mu^{j+1} times the integral of (\int H\,dx_m)^2 over |x'|\le(1-\varepsilon-|u|)/\mu. Since

\frac{(1-\varepsilon-|u|)(1+\varepsilon)}{1+\varepsilon-|u|}\le1-\varepsilon \iff -|u|(1+\varepsilon)\le-|u|(1-\varepsilon),
that region lies inside (1-\varepsilon)\mathcal{R}_{j-1}, so the slice contributes at most \mu^{j+1}M_{j,\varepsilon}I_j(H)=\mu M_{j,\varepsilon}I_j(F_u). For j=1 use Cauchy–Schwarz, which gives M_{1,\varepsilon}=1+\varepsilon. Integrate over u and use \mu\le1. Monotonicity in j follows by embedding: take F(s,u)=H(s/\mu_\delta)\mathbf 1_{[0,\delta]^{k-j}}(u) with \mu_\delta=1-(k-j)\delta/(1+\varepsilon) and let \delta\to0. ◻

Slicing naively gives each slice its own parameter \varepsilon'=\varepsilon/(1-|u|)\ge\varepsilon, which would only bound the weighted constant by \sup_{\varepsilon'\ge\varepsilon}M_{j,\varepsilon'}, a weaker statement. The single rescaling by \mu above avoids that loss.

Remark 11 (Computed, k=3). For the shifts (0,1,2) and D=1 (so \omega=2 and \alpha_D(c)=1 for both weight vectors), with unrestricted polynomials (degree 5 for k=3, degree 10 for k=2, cut off at |t|\le\rho for \rho\in\{1-\varepsilon,1,1+\varepsilon\}; values truncated):

\varepsilon M_{2,\varepsilon}\ge M_{3,\varepsilon}[(1,1,0)]\ge M_{3,\varepsilon}[(\frac12,1,\frac12)]\ge M_{3,\varepsilon}\ge
0 1.385933 1.349844 1.160121 1.646427
1/10 1.518566 1.482142 1.264697 1.793492
1/5 1.627161 1.585674 1.341926 1.882281
1/3 1.717103 1.663743 1.413309 1.917001

Both weighted columns stay below M_{2,\varepsilon}, as the proposition requires. The data are in the file notes/enlarged_pigeonhole_d5_d10.txt.

Remark 12 (scope). Neither proposition covers more general polytope supports [15, §3], Stadlmann’s hybrid supports [19], J-regions that depend on m, or arguments that are not linear in the indicators of primality. Those are Open.

Results of our own: how far the \varepsilon-enlargement alone can go

For the plain simplex the bound M_k\le\frac{k}{k-1}\log k shows where the method stops (k\ge51). This section starts the same analysis for the \varepsilon-enlarged simplex. For 0<\tau\le L put

M_k(L,\tau)=\sup_F\frac{\sum_{m=1}^k\int_{\tau\mathcal{R}_{k-1}}\left(\int_0^\infty F\,dt_m\right)^2\prod_{i\ne m}dt_i}{\int F^2},
the supremum over square-integrable F\ne0 supported on L\mathcal{R}_k. Then M_{k,\varepsilon}=M_k(1+\varepsilon,1-\varepsilon) and M_k=M_k(1,1). The quantity M_k(L,\tau) is non-decreasing in L and in \tau, satisfies M_k(cL,c\tau)=c\,M_k(L,\tau), and is non-decreasing in k by the embedding used in the proof of Proposition 10. Write S=t_1+\dots+t_k and call the index m active at t if S-t_m\le\tau.

Lemma 13 (Proved here; it is [15, Lemma 6.1] with the region of the marginals kept). Let \psi_1,\dots,\psi_k be measurable functions on L\mathcal{R}_k, positive almost everywhere, such that for almost every t'\in\tau\mathcal{R}_{k-1} and every m

\int_0^{L-|t'|}\frac{du}{\psi_m(t',u)}\le1,
where (t',u) is the point whose m-th coordinate is u and whose other coordinates are t'. Then
M_k(L,\tau)\le\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{t\in L\mathcal{R}_k}\ \sum_{m\text{ active at }t}\psi_m(t).

Proof. For t'\in\tau\mathcal{R}_{k-1} the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality gives

\left(\int F\,dt_m\right)^2\le\int\frac{dt_m}{\psi_m}\cdot\int\psi_mF^2\,dt_m\le\int\psi_mF^2\,dt_m .
Integrating over t'\in\tau\mathcal{R}_{k-1} gives the integral of \psi_mF^2 over the set where m is active. Sum over m. ◻

Proposition 14 (Proved here; elementary, possibly folklore). For k\ge2 and 0\le\varepsilon<1,

M_{k,\varepsilon}\le(1+\varepsilon)M_k\le(1+\varepsilon)\frac{k}{k-1}\log k .
Conversely, for 0\le\varepsilon\le M_k/(4k),
M_{k,\varepsilon}\ge(1+\varepsilon)M_k-\frac{64k^2}{M_k}\,\varepsilon^2 .
In particular \lim_{\varepsilon\to0^+}(M_{k,\varepsilon}-M_k)/\varepsilon=M_k.

Proof. By monotonicity in \tau and scaling, M_k(1+\varepsilon,1-\varepsilon)\le M_k(1+\varepsilon,1+\varepsilon)=(1+\varepsilon)M_k, and M_k\le\frac{k}{k-1}\log k is [15, Cor. 6.4].

For the lower bound, scaling gives M_{k,\varepsilon}=(1+\varepsilon)M_k(1,\rho) with \rho=(1-\varepsilon)/(1+\varepsilon). Put \delta=k(1-\rho) and let F on \mathcal{R}_k satisfy \sum_mJ_k^{(m)}(F)\ge(M_k-\eta)I_k(F). Split F=F_1+F_2 with F_2=F\mathbf 1_{S>\rho}, and let x=I_k(F_2)/I_k(F). Two facts follow from the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality on segments of length at most 1-\rho. First, a fiber of F_2 has length at most 1-\rho, so \sum_mJ_k^{(m)}(F_2)\le\delta\,I_k(F_2). Second, a fiber over t' with |t'|>\rho lies in \{S>\rho\} and has length at most 1-\rho, so restricting the marginals of F to \rho\mathcal{R}_{k-1} loses at most \delta\,I_k(F_2). By the triangle inequality for F\mapsto(\int F\,dt_m)_{m\le k} and the first fact,

M_k-\eta\le\left(\sqrt{M_k(1-x)}+\sqrt{\delta x}\right)^2\le M_k(1-x)+2\sqrt{M_k\delta x}+\delta x .
This is a quadratic inequality in \sqrt x. If \delta\le M_k/2 it gives x\le(16\delta+8\eta)/M_k. By the second fact the quotient of F for M_k(1,\rho) is at least M_k-\eta-\delta x. Letting \eta\to0 we get M_k(1,\rho)\ge M_k-16\delta^2/M_k. Finally \delta=2k\varepsilon/(1+\varepsilon), and \delta\le M_k/2 when \varepsilon\le M_k/(4k). ◻

So for fixed k the factor 1+\varepsilon is exact to first order, and no dual argument can improve it there. Any saving is an effect of large k: it needs \varepsilon of size about 1/k or more, where the shell \rho<\sigma\le1 carries most of the mass. The first inequality already restricts the parameter: with Bombieri–Vinogradov the criterion M_{k,\varepsilon}>4 forces \varepsilon>\varepsilon_0(k)=\frac{4(k-1)}{k\log k}-1 (Computed):

k 49 48 45 40 39 35 30
\varepsilon_0(k) 0.0068 0.0117 0.0274 0.0572 0.0638 0.0929 0.1368

For larger \varepsilon we use the lemma with a weight that changes shape where the indices start to become inactive.

Theorem 15 (Proved here for the inequality; Computed, interval arithmetic, for the numerical statement). Let k\ge2, 0<\tau<s_1\le L and a\ge s_1. Then

M_k(L,\tau)\le\sup_{0\le\sigma\le\tau}W(\sigma),\qquad W(\sigma)=\frac{ka}{k-1}\log\left(1+\frac{(k-1)(s_1-\sigma)}{a-\sigma}\right)+L-s_1+\sigma\log\frac{L-\sigma}{s_1-\sigma}.
Consequently M_{k,\varepsilon}<3.99 for every k\le29 and every 0\le\varepsilon<1. In particular the criterion of [15, Thm. 3.12] on the \varepsilon-enlarged simplex cannot give \mathrm{DHL}[k,2] for any k\le29 from the Bombieri–Vinogradov theorem, nor from any level of distribution \theta\le0.501, whatever the value of \varepsilon.

Proof. Fix \Lambda>0, put \lambda=\Lambda/(ka) and take the two-zone weight

\psi_m(t)=\lambda\,(a-S+kt_m)\quad(S\le s_1),\qquad \psi_m(t)=\Lambda\,\frac{t_m}{S}\quad(S>s_1).
It is positive wherever t_m>0. Where S\le s_1\le a every term is non-negative, so the sum over the active indices is at most the sum over all indices, which is \lambda(k(a-S)+kS)=\Lambda. Where S>s_1 the sum over the active indices is at most \Lambda\sum_mt_m/S=\Lambda. On the fiber over t' with |t'|=\sigma\le\tau we have S=\sigma+u, and
\int_0^{s_1-\sigma}\frac{du}{\lambda(a-\sigma+(k-1)u)}+\int_{s_1-\sigma}^{L-\sigma}\frac{(\sigma+u)\,du}{\Lambda u}=\frac{W(\sigma)}{\Lambda}.
Lemma 13 with \Lambda=\sup W gives the inequality.

For the numerical statement it suffices to treat k=29. For \varepsilon\le143/1000, Proposition 14 gives M_{29,\varepsilon}\le1.143\cdot\frac{29}{28}\log29<3.9863. The interval [143/1000,1] is cut into 857 intervals [e,e'] of length 1/1000. For each one the file code/cert_floor_k29.json lists rational a and s_1 with \sup W<3.99 for (L,\tau)=(1+e',1-e); by monotonicity in L and \tau this covers every \varepsilon\in[e,e']. The supremum over \sigma is bounded rigorously: the first logarithm is non-increasing in \sigma (because a\ge s_1) and the last term is non-decreasing, so on a piece [x,y] we bound W by the first term at x plus the last at y, in ball arithmetic at rational points, bisecting until the bound is below 3.99. The script is code/dual_eps_cert.py; a second script, code/check_floor_cert.py, re-checks the certificate with a different interval library. The largest value of \sup W over the 857 intervals is 3.9772 (floating point). ◻

Remark 16 (what was known, and a caution). Polymath8b state the uniform bound M_{k,\varepsilon}\le\frac{k}{k-1}\log(2k-1) for 0\le\varepsilon<1 [15, Prop. 6.5], and remark that the bounds for M_{k,\varepsilon} “do not appear to be as sharp”. That bound is below 4 exactly for k\le23, so Theorem 15 moves this floor from 24 to 30. It does not use their bound. We note, with caution, that we could not complete the last step of the proof of [15, Prop. 6.5] as we read it: the weights 1-S+kt_m are summed, and for S>1 their sum over the active indices can exceed k (at t=(s,0,\dots,0) with 1<s\le1+\varepsilon only the first index is active and its weight is 1+(k-1)s). Our reconstruction gives the constant \frac{k}{k-1}+\varepsilon in place of \frac{k}{k-1}. We re-read the proof in the current arXiv text: after the Cauchy–Schwarz step it says only “Integrating in t_1,\dots,t_{i-1}, t_{i+1},\dots,t_k and summing in i, we obtain the claim.” The weights 1-S+kt_i of the inactive indices can be negative when S>1, and dropping them is what lets the sum exceed k; it is at most k+(k-1)(S-1)\le k+(k-1)\varepsilon, which gives our constant. So the argument as printed proves M_{k,\varepsilon}\le\left(\frac{k}{k-1}+\varepsilon\right)\log(2k-1). Whether the stated inequality is nevertheless true is Open; we have not seen the journal version, and we may be missing an intended step. A brief search (the paper [15] and the Polymath wiki page on the variational problem) found no sharper published upper bound for M_{k,\varepsilon}; the search was not exhaustive, and Theorem 15 has not been reviewed by anyone outside this project.

Remark 17 (Computed (floating point): how far this weight goes). The best value of \sup W over (a,s_1), maximised over \varepsilon, is about 3.975 for k=29, 4.004 for k=30, 4.257 for k=40 and 4.459 for k=50, in each case near \varepsilon=0.24 to 0.28; the bound of [15, Prop. 6.5] is 4.187, 4.218, 4.482 and 4.689. For small \varepsilon the two-zone weight barely beats the scaling bound: at k=50 and \varepsilon=1/25 it gives 4.1458 against 4.1515 (earlier versions said “does not beat”; that was wrong). With a=519347/500000, s_1=244591/250000 this is certified (Computed, ball arithmetic, one program): M_{50,1/25}<4.1459 (code/twozone_50_cert.py, 1154 pieces). Polymath8b’s certified lower bound is 4.0043. The true threshold is presumably close to k=50, so the theorem is far from sharp. Script: code/dual_eps.py remark15.

Remark 18 (another weight). We also tried the weight \psi_m=\frac{\beta}{n}(a-S_A+nt_m), where n is the number of active indices at t and S_A the sum of the active coordinates, at points where m is active (set \psi_m=1 where m is inactive; the proof of Lemma 13 uses \psi_m only where m is active). It is positive for every a>\tau (an active index has t_m\ge S-\tau, so a-S_A+nt_m\ge a-\tau). Its sum over the active indices is exactly \beta a wherever some index is active, so by Lemma 13

M_k(L,\tau)\le a\sup_{t'\in\tau\mathcal{R}_{k-1}}I(a,t'),\qquad I(a,t')=\int_0^{L-|t'|}\frac{n\,du}{a-S_A+nu}.
An earlier version evaluated I only on special fibers and concluded wrongly that the weight is worse than scaling; Remark 19 settles the question.

Remark 19 (Proved here, with a computer-assisted step (Computed: ball arithmetic, two programs); possibly folklore). Let k\ge2, \tau<L, W=L-\tau, a>\tau, c_0=a-\tau, and for t'=(x_1,\dots,x_{k-1})\in\tau\mathcal{R}_{k-1} put s=\tau-|t'| and \nu=1+W^{-1}\sum_j\min(x_j,W). Then

I(a,t')\le G_a(s,\nu):=\frac{k}{k-1}\log\frac{c_0+ks}{c_0+s}+\int_0^W\frac{\nu\,dw}{c_0+\nu s+(\nu-1)w},
with equality when every non-zero x_j is at least W. Consequently, for k=50 and \varepsilon=1/25, with a=10373/10000,
M_{50,1/25}\le a\sup_{0\le s\le\tau,\ 1\le\nu\le1+(\tau-s)/W}G_a(s,\nu)<4.1463,
below the scaling bound (1+\varepsilon)\frac{k}{k-1}\log k=4.15153\ldots of Proposition 14, but above the bound 4.1459 that the two-zone weight of Theorem 15 gives (remark after Theorem 15). So this weight does not improve the best upper bound for M_{50,1/25} that we know.

Proof. Splitting the fiber. Index j is active at (t',u) iff x_j\ge u-s, and the denominator is a+\sum_{j\text{ active}}(u-x_j)\ge a-\tau>0. On u\in[0,s] all indices are active, the denominator is c_0+s+(k-1)u, and the integral is the logarithm in G_a. Write u=s+w with w\in(0,W] (the fiber has length L-|t'|=s+W). Then j is active iff x_j\ge w; let N(w)=\#\{j:x_j\ge w\} and M(w)=\int_0^wN=\sum_j\min(x_j,w). By the layer-cake formula \sum_{x_j\ge w}x_j=N(w)w+|t'|-M(w), so the denominator equals c_0+(1+N(w))s+M(w) and

I(a,t')=\frac{k}{k-1}\log\frac{c_0+ks}{c_0+s}+\int_0^W\frac{1+N(w)}{A+N(w)s+M(w)}\,dw,\qquad A=c_0+s.
