Equalising the profile. Let B=M(W) and \mu=B/W; we show that the last integral, J, is at most J_\mu=\int_0^W(1+\mu)/(A+\mu s+\mu w)\,dw, which is the second term of G_a(s,1+\mu). If \mu=0 both equal W/A. Otherwise let w_e=\min(W,\sup\{w:N(w)>0\}); N\ge1 on (0,w_e) and N=0 on (w_e,W]. The map \theta=M(w)/\mu is an increasing piecewise linear bijection [0,w_e]\to[0,W] with dw=\mu q\,d\theta, q=1/N, and q(\theta) is non-decreasing. With C(\theta)=A+\mu\theta and f_\theta(q)=q(q+1)/(C(\theta)q+s),
J=\frac{W}{A+B}+\mu\int_0^W\psi_\theta(q(\theta))\,d\theta,\qquad \psi_\theta(q)=f_\theta(q)-\frac{q}{A+B},
and the same identity with q\equiv q_0=1/\mu gives exactly J_\mu. One computes f_\theta'(q)=\frac1C+\frac{s(C-s)}{C(Cq+s)^2} and f_\theta''(q)=-\frac{2s(C-s)}{(Cq+s)^3}\le0, since C-s=c_0+\mu\theta>0. By concavity, J-J_\mu\le\mu\int_0^Wg\,h with g=\psi_\theta'(q_0) and h=q-q_0. Here g\ge\frac1C-\frac1{A+B}\ge0 because C\le A+\mu W=A+B; g is non-increasing in \theta, because \partial_C\frac{Cq^2+2sq+s}{(Cq+s)^2}=-\frac{Cq^3+3sq^2+2sq}{(Cq+s)^3}\le0; h is non-decreasing; and \int_0^Wh=(w_e-W)/\mu\le0. Chebyshev’s integral inequality gives \int gh\le W^{-1}\int g\int h\le0. Hence J\le J_\mu, which is the inequality. If every non-zero x_j is \ge W, then N is constant on (0,W], q\equiv q_0 and every step is an equality. Finally (\nu-1)W=M(W)\le|t'|=\tau-s.

The numerical step. On a cell [s_1,s_2]\times[\nu_1,\nu_2], the logarithm is increasing in s, the integrand of the second term is decreasing in s, and its \nu-derivative has the sign of c_0-w. So G_a\le\frac{k}{k-1}\log\frac{c_0+ks_2}{c_0+s_2}+\int_0^{c_0}[\nu_2,s_1]+\int_{c_0}^W[\nu_1,s_1] (here 0<c_0=0.0773<W=0.08), with closed-form integrals. Cells that miss the region are discarded by an exact rational test. Adaptive bisection of [0,\tau]\times[1,13] certifies a\,G_a<4.1463 on every cell in arb ball arithmetic (3.5\cdot10^6 cells; code/psi_weight_cert.py 10373 10000 41463 10000). A second program with a different library and subdivision (code/explore/psi_scratch_certify.py 10373 10000 4.14631, mpmath interval arithmetic) certifies <4.14631. ◻

Remark 20 (Computed (floating point): how sharp this is). The relaxed supremum (real \nu) at a=1.037 is about 4.1463525, at \nu\approx10.87, s\approx0.171, while the best fiber actually found is t'=((2\varepsilon)^{10},0^{39}) with a\,I=4.1463296; the gap is 2.3\cdot10^{-5}. So the weight’s true value lies between these two numbers at a=1.037. At a=1.0373 the floating-point maximum of a\,G_a is 4.1462991, so the certified 4.1463 has a margin of about 10^{-6}. The supremum over fibers is not always attained at ((2\varepsilon)^r,0,\dots): at a=1.036 the fiber (y^{10},0^{39}) with y=0.0811278>2\varepsilon beats every such fiber (a\,I larger by 4\cdot10^{-5}) (checked by exact piecewise integration and by two quadratures; code/explore/psi_scratch_checks.py). The improvement over scaling is small (0.0052), it is weaker than the two-zone weight’s 4.1459, and it is an upper bound for M_{50,1/25}, so it moves no bound on gaps. Its value is the reduction itself: the supremum over all 49-dimensional fibers reduces to two real parameters (Lemma “equalising the profile” in the proof), which may help with weights that see the active set. The only literature search behind “possibly folklore” is the brief one reported in the remark after Theorem 15 ([15] and the Polymath wiki page on the variational problem), plus one web search on 14 November 2026 that found no explicit numerical upper bound for M_{50,\varepsilon}; it is not exhaustive.

The limit of weights that see only t_m and S

Every weight used so far has the form \psi_m(t)=\Psi(S-t_m,t_m): Polymath8b’s \lambda(a-S+kt_m) and both zones of Theorem 15. Call this family \mathcal W. For weights in \mathcal W the problem “minimise the bound of Lemma 13” is convex: each fiber condition \int du/\Psi\le1 is convex in \Psi, and each point t gives a linear condition \sum_{m\text{ active}}\Psi(S-t_m,t_m)\le\Lambda. So its optimum can be approximated, and the approximation suggests how far a weight in \mathcal W can go.

Remark 21 (Computed (floating point), discretised; not a rigorous bound in either direction; Heuristic). Discretise \Psi=\rho\cdot(L-\sigma+(k-1)u) with \rho constant on cells of an N\times{}(geometric) grid in (S,u), impose the fiber conditions exactly on the grid \sigma\in\frac LN\mathbb Z, and impose the pointwise condition on all configurations of the grid of step L/N (found by a knapsack oracle). The convex optimum B_N(k,\varepsilon), computed by cutting planes with a floating-point conic solver (Clarabel) at each step, is:

(k,\varepsilon) N=80 N=120 N=160 best weight known M_{k,\varepsilon}\ge
(50,0) 3.9919 3.9919 (Cor. 6.4) 3.9274
(50,1/25) 4.1427 4.1420 4.1418 4.1458 (Thm. 15) 4.0043
(30,1/4) 4.0501 4.0345 4.0240 4.0021 (Thm. 15) –

At \varepsilon=0 the optimum over the whole family is the classical weight: the solver, started anywhere, returns \frac{k}{k-1}\log k. At (50,1/25) the discretised optimum settles near 4.14. This suggests (Heuristic) that no weight that sees only t_m and S can bring the dual bound much below 4.14 there, while the true value is at least 4.0043; but B_N is neither a restriction nor a relaxation of the problem over \mathcal W, and at (30,1/4) it exceeds the value 4.0021 of a known member of \mathcal W by about 0.022. At (30,1/4) the discretisation has not converged (the values still fall, and lie above the continuum value \le4.0021 that Theorem 15 already certifies), so we draw no conclusion about the exact floor of \mathcal W; runs at k=31,32 gave B_{120}=4.0261 to 4.0976, also unconverged. The lesson, heuristically, is the first one: to approach the truth near k=50, a dual weight must depend on more of t than t_m and S. The optimal weight is known in principle: if F>0 is the extremal function, \psi_m=\int F\,dt_m/F makes Lemma 13 an equality (the Collatz–Wielandt principle for the positive operator F\mapsto\sum_{m\text{ active}}\int F\,dt_m). A first test (Computed (floating point), 20000 random points, not a bound; code/cw_sample.py) is encouraging on the plain simplex and discouraging on the enlarged one. For k=3, \varepsilon=0 and the degree-12 Galerkin function (M_3\ge1.646440) the sampled ratio stays in [1.6464,1.6465], which would be an almost sharp upper bound if the supremum could be certified. For \varepsilon>0 the Galerkin polynomial changes sign (\min F/\max F=-0.0003 at (k,\varepsilon,d)=(10,1/5,12), -0.11 at (50,1/25,12)). This is structural: where no index is active the extremal function vanishes, so near that region any positive F makes the ratio large. Making the principle usable there is Open. The script is code/dual_lp.py; runs/dual_lp_conv.log and runs/dual_lp_floor.log hold the output, including the solver’s warning “Solution may be inaccurate” on some of the runs in the table; the N=80 values at (50,1/25) and (50,0) are not in these logs. The last column is from Table 2 and Theorem 6.

Remark 22 (heuristic: does the enlarged simplex stop at k=50?). An Aitken extrapolation of the certified values of Table 2 in the degree (code/extrapolate_eps.py; tested at k=50, where it is optimistic by 0.0001–0.0012) gives limits 3.9913–3.9938 at k=49, and the grid \varepsilon\le1/20 at D\le20 gives at most 3.995. This suggests, but does not prove (Open), that the \varepsilon-enlarged simplex with Bombieri–Vinogradov stops at k=50; with Theorem 15 it would bracket the floor of this method between 30 and 50.

Remark 23 (scope). Theorem 15 concerns the \varepsilon-enlarged simplex only. It says nothing about the 2026 claims, which use other supports and new equidistribution estimates. A floor for those is the Problem in the next section.

Stadlmann-type supports

This section takes the first steps on the next rung, Stadlmann’s region for H_1\le240 [19], and gives a first rigorous answer to the Problem in the Programme (Section 7) for the one-band supports used by [19] and [3]. For this version a helper re-read both sources (the arXiv text of [19], the draft [3]); our notes with page references are in notes/stadlmann-recheck-s5.md.

The support and the criterion

The following is Literature (preprint), read in [19, Def. 1, Def. 5, Prop. 1]; the implication from the criterion to \mathrm{DHL}[k,2] is Claimed. For \delta>0, -\varepsilon=A_0<A_1<\dots<A_n<\frac12-\varepsilon and caps \delta<B_{j,m}\le B_{j,m+1}\le B_{j,m}+\delta, the support T_k(\delta,\underline A,\underline B,\varepsilon) is the set of t\in[0,1]^k such that, for the j with \sum_it_i\in[A_{j-1}+\varepsilon,A_j+\varepsilon), one has \sum_{i\in I}t_i\le B_{j,|I|} where I=\{i:t_i>\delta\} (the “large” coordinates). With one band (n=1), the functional J(F) of [19] is the integral of \left(\int F\,dt_k\right)^2 over t_1+\dots+t_{k-1}\le A_1-\varepsilon (our reduction of the general definition), I(F)=\int_{T_k}F^2, and the criterion is k(1-c_1)J(F)-kc_2K(F)>I(F) for a symmetric F supported on T_k, where c_1,c_2\ge0 come from the minorant and K(F)\ge0. Stadlmann’s choice is k=49, \varepsilon=0.0075, A_1=0.253, \delta=0.028, B_{1,1}=B_{1,2}=0.15, B_{1,m}=0.17 (m\ge3) and c_1=c_2=0; that of [3] is k=45, \varepsilon=1/125, A_1=257/1000, \delta=41/2500 with caps listed in their Table 3. Both use one band. The equidistribution estimates behind the criterion are Claimed; we have not checked them.

In the Polymath scaling u=4t the support lies in L\mathcal{R}_k with L=4(A_1+\varepsilon), the marginals are integrated over \tau\mathcal{R}_{k-1} with \tau=4(A_1-\varepsilon), and the criterion with c_1=c_2=0 reads kJ/I>4. Write s=(L+\tau)/2=4A_1 and e=(L-\tau)/(L+\tau)=\varepsilon/A_1; the scaling M_k(cL,c\tau)=cM_k(L,\tau) of Section 4 gives

M_k(L,\tau)=s\,M_k(1+e,1-e)=4A_1\,M_{k,\varepsilon/A_1}.\qquad(2)
For Stadlmann, (L,\tau)=(1.042,0.982), s=253/250, e=15/506; for [3], (L,\tau)=(1.06,0.996), s=257/250, e=8/257.

Without the caps

Proposition 24 (Computed, exact rational arithmetic). With even power sums, M_{49,15/506}\ge3.9685534 (degree 20, 707 coefficients) and M_{45,8/257}\ge3.9029887 (degree 21, 846 coefficients). Hence, by (2), M_{49}(1.042,0.982)\ge4.016176 and M_{45}(1.06,0.996)\ge4.012272.

The two certificates, in the directory code/, are cert_stad49_capfree_D20e.json and cert_ax45_capfree_D21e.json; the script verify_cert.py re-checks them. Our degree-21 basis spans the space \mathcal B_{21} of [3, Thm. 11.1] (Literature (preprint)): their §11 defines \mathcal B_{21} as the span of (L-P_1)^aP_\lambda with P_\lambda a product of even power sums and a+|\lambda|\le21, with the same L=53/50 (definition quoted in notes/sources-enlarged-supports.md), which is our family; its dimension, 846, agrees with theirs. On their capped support they report the exact value 4.0043840983\ldots (Claimed); on the larger cap-free region, with the same space, we get 4.012272. Both are lower bounds, for M_T and for M_{45}(1.06,0.996)\ge M_T respectively, so the comparison proves nothing; but it suggests (if their value is right) that at this degree the caps cost about 0.008. This is a comparison, not a verification of [3].

With the caps

For F=P\,1_T with P a polynomial, write I(P), J(P) for the cap-free functionals on L\mathcal{R}_k (marginals over \tau\mathcal{R}_{k-1}) and V=L\mathcal{R}_k\setminus T (Polymath scaling; g=4\delta, C_m=4B_{1,m}).

Proposition 25 (Proved here; elementary). Let R_m(g,c)=\{u\in L\mathcal{R}_k:\ u_1,\dots,u_m>g,\ u_1+\dots+u_m\ge c\}, put c_m=C_m for m\le6 and c_7=7g. Then, with

E(P)=\sum_{m=1}^{7}\binom km\int_{R_m(g,c_m)}P^2 ,
one has I_T(F)\le I(P) and, for every 0<\eta<1,
J_T(F)\ \ge\ \max\left(J(P)-2\sqrt{J(P)\,L\,E(P)},\ (1-\eta)J(P)-(\eta^{-1}-1)L\,E(P)\right).

Proof. A point of V has a large set I with \sum_{i\in I}u_i>C_{|I|}. If |I|\le6 it lies in a copy of R_{|I|}(g,c_{|I|}); if |I|\ge7, any seven of its large coordinates exceed g and sum to more than 7g, so it lies in a copy of R_7(g,c_7). By symmetry of P^2, \int_VP^2\le E(P). On the fiber over u' write a=\int P\,du_k and b=\int P\,1_V\,du_k, so that the marginal of F is a-b. Cauchy–Schwarz gives b^2\le L\int P^21_V\,du_k, hence \int b^2\le L\,E(P). Then (a-b)^2\ge a^2-2|a||b| with Cauchy–Schwarz once more, or (a-b)^2\ge(1-\eta)a^2-(\eta^{-1}-1)b^2. Finally I_T(F)=\int_TP^2\le I(P). ◻

The integrals over R_m(g,c) are again exact rational numbers for the basis (L-P_1)^aP^b (Proved here; code/caps_exact.py). Write the first m coordinates as g+z. Integrating out the other k-m coordinates with Lemma 3 leaves powers of L-mg-|z|; the shifted power sums are P_j(g+z)=\sum_l\binom jlg^{j-l}P_l(z) with P_0=m and P_1=|z|; at fixed r=|z| the integral of \prod_lP_l(z)^{f_l} is r^{m-1+w(f)}E_m(f)/(m-1+w(f))! (now with odd parts as well); and the remaining integral in r over [\max(c-mg,0),L-mg] is elementary. The code reproduces Lemma 3 exactly when g=c=0, and agrees with direct symbolic integration (sympy) for k=3, m=1,2, g=1/10.

Since all three quantities are exact quadratic forms in the coefficients of P, we choose P as the top eigenvector of (1-\eta)J-(\eta^{-1}-1)L\,G_E against I, where G_E is the Gram matrix of E, and evaluate both bounds exactly. The penalty works: at degree 16 it lowers E/I from 3.2\cdot10^{-4} (for the cap-free optimiser) to 3.4\cdot10^{-7}.

Proposition 26 (Computed, exact rational arithmetic). For Stadlmann’s parameters (k=49) there is a polynomial P of degree 20 in even power sums (707 coefficients) with

\frac{kJ_T(P\,1_T)}{I_T(P\,1_T)}\ \ge\ 3.95972 .
At degree 16 (307 coefficients) the same method gives 3.93625.

This is below 4, and at first the rung 240 was therefore not reproduced; Theorem 28 below supersedes it. The certificate is code/cert_stad49_caps_D20e.json, written by code/stadlmann_caps_opt.py; the best bound came from \eta=1/800, with E/I=1.6\cdot10^{-7} and the bound of Proposition 25 in its square-root form. Where the loss comes from (Computed, same runs): the cap-free optimiser of degree 20 has s\,kJ/I=4.01618 but E/I=1.2\cdot10^{-3} (of which 1.2\cdot10^{-4} from points with one coordinate above 0.6); forcing E down costs about 0.045 of the cap-free ratio (4.0161\to3.9710), and the Cauchy–Schwarz step costs another 0.011. Both losses come from the way we bound J_T, not from the caps as such: F=P\,1_T is cut off on V anyway, and Proposition 25 charges for P on V only because it does not compute the capped marginals. The next step is therefore an exact formula for J_T(P\,1_T), which needs the fibers of T and not only integrals over V. A first attempt that dropped the condition u_i>g in the union bound failed badly (E/I\approx146 for the cap-free optimiser; runs/stad49_caps_D20.log).

The capped functionals exactly

Version 0.5 bounded J_T(P\,1_T) through Proposition 25, and the Cauchy–Schwarz step there turned out to be the whole difficulty. We now compute I_T(P\,1_T) and J_T(P\,1_T) exactly. Notation as above: u in the Polymath scaling, R=L-|u'| on the fiber over u'\in\mathbb R_{\ge0}^{k-1}, I(u)=\{i:u_i>g\}, m(u)=|I(u)|, s(u)=\sum_{i\in I(u)}u_i, and Q(u',x)=\int_0^xP(u',t)\,dt.

Proposition 27 (Proved here; elementary). Assume C_{m+1}\le C_m+g for all m (this is Stadlmann’s condition B_{j,m+1}\le B_{j,m}+\delta) and g\ge L-\tau (this holds for every parameter set below: L-\tau=0.06 against g=0.112 and 0.0728 for [19, 8], and L-\tau=0.064 against g=0.0656 for [3]). Call u' admissible if s(u')\le C_{m(u')}. Then:

  1. the fiber \{t\ge0:(u',t)\in T\} is empty if u' is not admissible, and otherwise equals [0,U(u')] with U=\min\left(R,\max(g,\,C_{m(u')+1}-s(u'))\right);

  2. one has I_T(P\,1_T)=I(P)-\int_VP^2 and

    J_T(P\,1_T)=J(P)-\mathrm{Corr}_1-\mathrm{Corr}_2,
    where \mathrm{Corr}_1=\int Q(u',R)^2 over the non-admissible u' with |u'|\le\tau, and \mathrm{Corr}_2=\int\left(Q(u',R)^2-Q(u',U)^2\right) over the admissible u' with U<R;

  3. each of the three correction integrals is a finite signed sum, over the size m of the large set and over j\ge0 further coordinates forced above g (inclusion–exclusion for “all other coordinates are at most g”, with weight (-1)^j\binom nm\binom{n-m}{j}), of integrals over polygons in the plane of r_A=s-mg and \rho=|u|-s-jg. On each polygon the integrand, after the shape integrals of Lemma 3 and of Proposition 25, is a polynomial in (r_A,\rho), so every term is an exact rational number.

For Stadlmann’s parameters every correction piece has m\le9 and j\le3.

Proof. (1) If t\le g the large set of (u',t) is I(u'), so (u',t)\in T iff u' is admissible and |u'|+t\le L. If t>g the large set is I(u')\cup\{k\} and the condition is s(u')+t\le C_{m(u')+1}; since C_{m+1}\le C_m+g, this condition with t>g implies s(u')<C_{m(u')}, so a non-admissible u' has an empty fiber. Hence for admissible u' the fiber is [0,\min(R,g)]\cup(g,\min(R,C_{m+1}-s)], which is [0,U]. (2) F=P\,1_T has marginal Q(u',U) on admissible u' and 0 otherwise, while the cap-free marginal is Q(u',R); subtract. (3) By symmetry, integrate over the points whose large set is exactly A=\{1,\dots,m\} and multiply by \binom nm; the condition that the remaining coordinates are at most g is \prod(1-1_{u_i>g}), expanded. On such a piece put z=u-g on the m+j forced coordinates; at fixed r_A=|z_A| and \rho=|z_B|+|y| (y the free coordinates) the integral of a product of power sums is a sum over splits of the product of the A-group density of Proposition 25 and the convolution of the j shifted coordinates with the free ones, which is again of Dirichlet type. The integrand (P^2, Q(R)^2, Q(g)^2 or Q(x)^2 with x=C_{m+1}-s) is a polynomial in R and x, both affine in (r_A,\rho). The regions are: r_A\ge C_m-mg and r_A+\rho\le const (the non-admissible pieces); r_A\ge C_{m+1}-(m+1)g and R>g (where U=g); and the rectangle r_A<C_{m+1}-(m+1)g, \rho<L-C_{m+1}-jg (where U=C_{m+1}-s). In the last two cases U<R forces R>g, so |u'|<L-g\le\tau by the hypothesis g\ge L-\tau, and these fibers lie in the domain of J. In every piece the large set carries at least C_1=0.6 or the free mass is below L-C_1, so jg<0.442, j\le3; and mg<L gives m\le9. These one-line inequalities are Proved here; their integer-unit analogues, for lists of natural numbers in units of 1/1000, are Lean-checked (stadlmann_extra_le_three, stadlmann_large_count). ◻

Theorem 28 (Computed, exact rational arithmetic, two independent programs). Let P be the cap-free optimiser of degree 20 of Proposition 24 (707 coefficients, even power sums). For Stadlmann’s parameters (k=49, L=1.042, \tau=0.982, g=0.112, C_1=C_2=0.6, C_m=0.68 for m\ge3) and F=P\,1_T,

\frac{k\,J_T(F)}{I_T(F)}=4.0120389035\ldots\ >\ 4 .

The polynomial, in u-coordinates, is code/cap6/P_capfree_D20_u.json (written from the certificate code/cert_stad49_capfree_D20e.json by code/to_ucoords.py; the cap-free value kJ/I it gives equals \frac{253}{250} times the certified Rayleigh quotient exactly). Two programs compute I_T and J_T as exact rationals and agree on both numbers digit for digit: code/capped_exact.py (ours; Proposition 27, “cap-free minus corrections”, 46 minutes on three cores) and code/capped_check.py, written by a helper from the definitions only, without access to our code, which sums directly over the exact large set (no cap-free term at all) with exponential-moment generating functions (6 minutes on four cores). The checks behind them: the direct and the corrected I_T in capped_exact.py agree exactly; a Monte Carlo test at k=12, D=4, where the caps remove 47\% of I and 45\% of J, agrees to within one standard deviation (code/cap6/mc_test.py); and capped_check.py agrees exactly with a brute-force evaluation by polytope triangulation and symbolic integration for k=2,\dots,5 with caps that bite (code/capped_check_tests.py). Results: code/cap6/res_capfree_D20.json, runs/capped_check_D20.json.

The caps cost only 0.0041: relative to the cap-free values, \int_VP^2=1.209\cdot10^{-3}\,I(P), and \mathrm{Corr}_1, \mathrm{Corr}_2 on the pieces U=g and U=C_{m+1}-s are 1.72\cdot10^{-4}, 8.1\cdot10^{-5} and 1.98\cdot10^{-3} of J(P). The largest loss is the cut of the fiber at C_{m+1}-s (for m=0: no coordinate above 0.6). So Proposition 26 (3.9597) measured the weakness of Proposition 25, not the caps; it is superseded. The loss grows with the degree of the cap-free optimiser (Computed, exact: capped_check.py for every degree; the degree-20 value also with capped_exact.py, and the degree-8 value too, though that run’s output is not stored in the repository):

degree 8 12 16 20
cap-free kJ/I 3.789069 3.923943 3.987180 4.016176
capped kJ_T/I_T 3.789068 3.923849 3.985976 4.012039

so higher-degree optimisers put more mass near the caps; optimising P on T itself (Proposition 34) can only raise the capped value. At k=45 this is consistent with the comparison after Proposition 24, where the caps of [3] appeared to cost about 0.008.

Corollary 29 (conditional; the analytic input is Claimed). The variational step of [19] is independently reproduced: the criterion of [19, Prop. 1] holds for k=49 with his support, his caps and his parameters, and c_1=c_2=0. Together with the Lean-checked admissible 49-tuple of diameter 240 (Theorem 1), H_1\le240 then follows from his reduction [19, Prop. 1] and his equidistribution theorem, neither of which we have verified.

This is a reproduction of part of a claimed result, not a new bound, and it says nothing about the correctness of the new equidistribution estimates of [19] (his Lemmas 3–7 and Prop. 3), on which everything rests. Our formulation agrees with [19] as re-read in our notes (notes/stadlmann-recheck-s5.md), with these differences, none of which changes the value: we use the scaling u=4t (criterion kJ/I>4); we use our own reduction of his J for one band (the band conditions on t_k and t_k' follow from the support of F); we ignore his closed versus half-open band endpoints (measure zero); and we use our own test space (even power sums times powers of L-P_1, degree 20) instead of his \mathcal B_{19} or \mathcal B_{21}, whose coefficients he does not print.

Remark 30 (Computed, interval arithmetic: a ceiling, our own). Every coordinate of a point of T is at most 0.6 (Proved here, by the same linear inequality; an integer-unit analogue in units of 1/1000 is Lean-checked: stadlmann_coord_le) and T\subset L\mathcal{R}_k, so the capped quantity is at most M_{49}(1.042,0.982), which Theorem 15 with a=1.0417, s_1=1.0007 bounds by 4.14 (certified with dual_eps_cert.certify_box, 337 pieces). Hence 4.0120<\sup_F kJ_T/I_T\le4.14. Cutting the fibers of the two-zone weight at 0.6 lowers its floating-point ceiling only from 4.13785 to 4.13753: the supremum of W sits near \sigma=\tau, where the fibers are short anyway, so a ceiling that sees the caps needs a weight that sees the large coordinates (Open).

The next rung: the support of [3]

The same computation applies to the one-band support of [3] (k=45, H(45)=212), whose caps change with m. In the Polymath scaling their Table 3 gives L=53/50, \tau=249/250, g=41/625 and C_m=4B_{1,m} with B_{1,m}=\frac{1}{5000}(777,794,875,917,953,983,1016,1042,1063,1081) for m\le10 and B_{1,m}=1081/5000 for m\ge11 (Literature (preprint), read in [3]; our notes record the page). These caps satisfy \delta<B_{1,m}\le B_{1,m+1}\le B_{1,m}+\delta (Lean-checked: charton_caps_valid), and g=0.0656\ge L-\tau=0.064. At most 16 coordinates can exceed g, and every correction piece of Proposition 27 has j\le6 (Proved here; the integer-unit analogues in units of 1/1250 are Lean-checked: charton_large_count, charton_extra_le_six).

Proposition 31 (Computed, exact rational arithmetic, two independent programs). Let P be our cap-free optimiser of degree 21 for M_{45}(1.06,0.996) (Proposition 24, 846 coefficients) and T the support of [3] with the caps of their Table 3. Then

\frac{45\,J_T(P\,1_T)}{I_T(P\,1_T)}=4.0027351128\ldots\ >\ 4 .

The value was computed by code/capped_check.py (13 minutes on four cores; input code/cap6/P_ax45_D21_u.json, result code/cap6/check_ax45_D21.json). Our own program code/capped_exact.py (Proposition 27, now with caps depending on m; 108 minutes on four cores, 228 pieces, result code/cap6/res_ax45_D21.json) gives the same I_T and J_T as exact rationals. Relative to the cap-free values, \int_VP^2=2.67\cdot10^{-3}I(P) and the three J corrections are 3.0\cdot10^{-4}, 9.6\cdot10^{-5} and 4.65\cdot10^{-3} of J(P). The cap-free value of the same P is 4.012272, so the caps cost 0.0095 here, more than at k=49: g is smaller (0.0656 against 0.112), so more coordinates count as large, and the largest correction, the cut at C_{m+1}-s, is 4.65\cdot10^{-3} of J(P) against 1.98\cdot10^{-3}. The value reported in [3, Thm. 11.1], with their own optimised F in the same polynomial space, is 4.00438 (Claimed); ours is lower, as it should be for a vector optimised without the caps, and it already exceeds 4.

The draft [3] is not consistent about its caps: its Lemma 9.1 states C(1)=C(2)=31/50 and C(s)=17/25 for s\ge3. With those caps the same P gives

\frac{45\,J_T(P\,1_T)}{I_T(P\,1_T)}=3.9736869862\ldots\ <\ 4
(Computed, exact, capped_check.py, code/cap6/check_ax45_D21_lemma91caps.json). This does not show that the criterion fails for those caps: our P was optimised without caps, and the caps of Lemma 9.1 are strictly smaller than those of Table 3 for every m\ge1, so their support is smaller. But it does show that, with this test function, the conclusion depends on which of the two cap sets the draft intends, and that Table 3 is the one under which the criterion is easy to confirm. Within our 846-dimensional space a test function optimised on the smaller support does not reach 3.99 (Proposition 35); whether a test function outside that space can work with Lemma 9.1’s caps is Open.

We do not lower the project’s bound to 212 in this version. The analytic input of [3] (its equidistribution statements at level \omega=7/1000 for moduli generated by these caps) is Claimed, the draft is not consistent about which caps it uses, and with the other cap set no test function in our space reaches 3.99 (Proposition 35). What is established here is narrower: for the caps of Table 3 of [3], the variational criterion of [3, Thm. 4.10] holds at k=45 (Computed, two programs). Together with the Lean-checked 45-tuple of diameter 212, this would give H_1\le212 conditionally on the analytic input of [3] for those caps; whether that input covers the caps of Table 3 is the question an expert reader of [3] should settle first.

Remark 32 (Computed, interval arithmetic: ceiling for k=45). As in Remark 30, Theorem 15 with a=1.0596, s_1=1.0166 gives M_{45}(1.06,0.996)\le4.126 (495 pieces). So the capped value of [3] lies in (4.0027,\,4.126]. Cutting the fibers at the largest coordinate C_1=0.6216 moves the floating-point ceiling of this weight only from 4.12481 to 4.12446.

Optimising the test function on the capped support

At first every capped value came from a polynomial optimised without the caps and then truncated to T. Both capped functionals are quadratic forms in the coefficients c of P: I_T(P\,1_T)=c^{\top}G_Ic and J_T(P\,1_T)=c^{\top}G_Jc.

Remark 33 (Computed; a description of the method, validated by the exact self-tests below, not a proved statement). In the direct method of code/capped_check.py, I_T is a linear function of the coefficient products \kappa_I[F](y)=\sum_{F_1+F_2=F}p_{F_1}(y)p_{F_2}(y) and J_T a linear function of \kappa_J[F](y,v)=\sum_{F_1+F_2=F}q_{F_1}q_{F_2}, where P=\sum_Fp_F(y)\prod_jP_j^{F_j}, y=L-P_1, and q_F(y,v) are the coefficients of \int_0^vP\,dt. Running the chain of exact linear maps of that program backwards gives exact weights w_I[F][\alpha]=\partial I_T/\partial\kappa_I[F][y^\alpha] and w_J[F][(b,\gamma)]=\partial J_T/\partial\kappa_J[F][y^bv^\gamma], and then G_I[t_1,t_2]=w_I[F_1+F_2][a_1+a_2] for basis terms t_i=(L-P_1)^{a_i}\prod_jP_j^{F_{i,j}}, and G_J=Q^{\top}HQ with H[(F_1,b_1,\gamma_1),(F_2,b_2,\gamma_2)]=w_J[F_1+F_2][(b_1+b_2,\gamma_1+\gamma_2)] and Q the (sparse, explicit) map from basis terms to the q_F.

The reverse pass costs about one forward evaluation (code/capped_gram.py). Its self-test is exact: for the input polynomial, \sum\langle\kappa_I,w_I\rangle and \sum\langle\kappa_J,w_J\rangle equal the I_T and J_T of capped_check.py as rationals (passed at k=12, at k=49 with the polynomial of Theorem 28, and at k=45 with the polynomial and caps of Proposition 35). G_I is exact; G_J is assembled in fixed point with an explicit entrywise error bound. The matrices are only used to find a vector, or, with that error bound, to certify that \sigma G_I-kG_J is positive definite (recursive Schur complements in ball arithmetic), i.e. that no P in the whole test space exceeds \sigma; every capped value stated as a certificate is recomputed from the rounded vector by the exact evaluators.

Proposition 34 (Computed, exact rational arithmetic, two programs). For Stadlmann’s support and parameters (k=49, as in Theorem 28) let P^\ast be the top generalised eigenvector of (k\,G_J,G_I) in the same 707-dimensional space (even power sums, degree 20), rounded to 400 significant bits per coefficient. Then

\frac{k\,J_T(P^\ast1_T)}{I_T(P^\ast1_T)}=4.0124198475\ldots\ >\ 4 .

Files: code/cap6/P_opt49_D20r_u.json (vector), code/cap6/check_opt49_D20r.json (capped_check.py, 7 minutes on two cores), and code/cap6/res_opt49_D20r.json (capped_exact.py, 60 minutes on two cores); the two programs agree on I_T and J_T as exact rationals. The unrounded vector gives the same value to twelve digits (check_opt49_D20.json). The cap-free value of P^\ast is 4.0158303, against 4.0161761 for the cap-free optimiser.

So optimising on T regains only 0.00038 of the 0.0041 that truncation cost (about 9\%): in this test space the margin of the variational step of [19] is 0.0124, and the caps cost 0.0038 even for the best polynomial of degree 20.

Proposition 35 (Computed, ball arithmetic, certified at \sigma=3.99; one program’s Gram weights, checked at one random point against both exact programs (a probabilistic Schwartz–Zippel check, not a second derivation)). Let T' be the support of [3] at k=45 with the caps of their Lemma 9.1 (C_1=C_2=31/50, C_m=17/25 for m\ge3; L=53/50, \tau=249/250, g=41/625), and let V be our 846-dimensional space (even power sums times powers of L-P_1, degree \le21). Then 3.99\,G_I-45\,G_J is positive definite, so

\frac{45\,J_{T'}(P\,1_{T'})}{I_{T'}(P\,1_{T'})}<3.99<4\qquad\text{for every }0\ne P\in V .

The weights w_I,w_J of Remark 33 were computed exactly (88 minutes on two cores). G_J was assembled with absolute error at most 2^{-P} per weight, and the resulting entrywise error bound was put into the balls. Positive definiteness was certified by recursive Schur complements in ball arithmetic: for \sigma=4 with P=5000 (so 4800-bit balls in the Schur step, as the code uses P-200; command recorded in the project’s handoff file) and halving blocks, and again for \sigma=4 and \sigma=3.99 with –prec 6500 and blocks split 1:2 (code/capped_gram.py –ceiling 4/1,3990/1000 –split 1/3, log runs/gram45_lemma91_recheck.log).

How the weights were checked. Both c\mapsto c^{\top}G_Ic and the map c\mapsto I_{T'} computed by capped_check.py are polynomials of degree 2 in the 846 coefficients, and likewise for J. If they differed, their difference would be a nonzero polynomial of degree at most 2. By the Schwartz–Zippel lemma it then vanishes at a uniformly random point of S^{846}, |S|=2^{32}+1, with probability at most 2/|S|<2^{-31}. At the random integer vector code/cap6/P_rand45_lemma91_u.json (seed recorded in the file) the weights reproduce I_{T'} and J_{T'} of capped_check.py exactly (runs/gram45_rand_selftest.log, code/cap6/check_rand45_lemma91.json). The ball forms c^{\top}G_Ic and c^{\top}G_Jc contain these exact values. So the proposition rests on capped_check.py (which agrees exactly with the independent capped_exact.py at Stadlmann’s and at Charton et al.’s Table 3 parameters, Theorem 28 and Proposition 31) up to a failure probability below 2^{-31} for each functional. The independent capped_exact.py finished evaluating the same random point (all 273 pieces, resumed from a checkpoint; runs/exact_rand45.log): its I_{T'} and J_{T'} equal those of capped_check.py exactly (code/cap6/res_rand45_lemma91.json, compare_rand45_lemma91.out), so capped_check.py is also cross-checked at these caps, with the same failure probability. The exact weights used for both tests were loaded from a file outside the repository and are not yet archived.

The optimum. Inverse iteration in V converges to 45J_{T'}/I_{T'}=3.9855478 (code/cap6/P_opt45_lemma91_D21r_u.json, the eigenvector rounded to 400 bits; Computed (floating point), a value from the Gram matrices, not re-certified exactly). So in V the truncated cap-free vector (3.97369) is beaten by 0.0119, the best possible lies (up to that floating-point evaluation) in [3.98554,\,3.99), and 4 is out of reach.

Since our space V is the space \mathcal B_{21} of [3, Thm. 11.1] (see after Proposition 24), this suggests that the caps of Lemma 9.1 cannot give their 4.00438, and that the value they report belongs to the caps of their Table 3, for which our truncated vector already gives 4.0027 (Proposition 31). The ceiling rests on one program and a randomised check, and we have not compared their support and fiber conventions with ours in every detail, so this is a located discrepancy for the authors or an expert reader, not a refutation.

Remark 36 (a failed prediction, our own). We had predicted (notes/session8-prediction.md) that re-optimisation would regain about a tenth of the truncation loss, as at k=49; it regains 31\% (0.0119 of 0.0386), and a second-order model would predict nearly all of it. Both heuristics are ruled out as quantitative tools.

The rung 236: the support of Kintali

[8] uses Stadlmann’s criterion with one band at k=48 and the support “D182” (Literature (preprint), read in [8, §2]): \varepsilon=3/400, A=253/1000, \delta=91/5000 and a single cap B_m=1899/10000 for every m\ge1. In our scaling this is L=1.042 and \tau=0.982 (Stadlmann’s values), g=91/1250 and C_m=1899/2500 for all m; it satisfies C_{m+1}\le C_m+g and g\ge L-\tau, so our evaluators apply unchanged (code/to_ucoords.py –params kintali). At most ten coordinates of a point of this support exceed g, and every correction piece has j\le3 (Proved here: in the cap-violation pieces and in the \mathrm{Corr}_2 pieces alike, jg<L-C_1=0.2824, while 4g=0.2912; integer-unit analogues in units of 1/2500 are Lean-checked: kintali_large_count, and kintali_extra_le_three for the cap-violation pieces only). The analytic input is Stadlmann’s (his Prop. 3, with the Type IIc range made empty); the test function is a public 1295-term witness of degree 25 (the k=50 certificate of the AxiomMath PrimeGapsLib repository, according to [8, §3]), transported by the dilation z=\frac{26}{25U}t and used at k=48 without re-optimising; the claimed values are 48J/I=1.0007747\ldots in his normalisation, i.e. 4.0030988 in ours, and 4.0036348 without the caps (Claimed). The second 236 preprint [18], whose full text was supplied to us later, uses the same support with the same witness (Literature (preprint), read in [18, §4]; notes in notes/sources-236-song-yue.md). Its criterion divides by the cap-free denominator, 48J(F)/I_0>2001/2000. The authors state that a fresh regeneration of their capped numerator is still pending. An exact recomputation of it from their table is a natural next step for us.

Proposition 37 (Computed, exact rational arithmetic, two independent programs). Let P be our cap-free optimiser of degree 22 for M_{48,15/506} (1041 coefficients, even power sums; certified Rayleigh quotient 3.9589046383, so kJ/I=\frac{253}{250}\cdot3.9589046=4.0064115 without caps), rounded to 400 significant bits per coefficient, and T the support of [8] above. Then

\frac{48\,J_T(P\,1_T)}{I_T(P\,1_T)}=4.0060814208\ldots\ >\ 4 .

Computed by code/capped_check.py (22 minutes on four cores; input code/cap6/P_kin48_D22r_u.json, result code/cap6/check_kin48_D22r.json; certificate code/cert_kin48_capfree_D22e.json). Our second program, capped_exact.py (cap-free value minus 176 inclusion–exclusion pieces, checkpointed; 15.5 core-hours in two segments: it was resumed at 77 of 176 pieces after a worker restart, and the second segment took 2.1 hours on four cores), gave the same I_T and J_T as exact rationals (code/cap6/res_kin48_D22r.json, code/compare_two_programs.py, code/cap6/compare_kin48_D22r.json). At degree 20 the same construction stays below 4 (3.9994 even without caps).

Kintali’s caps are generous: they cost our vector only 0.00033, against 0.0041 for Stadlmann’s caps at k=49. Our value is above his claimed 4.0031, which is plausible because his witness was optimised for k=50 and a different support and then transported, while ours is optimised for this k and this (L,\tau). So the variational step of [8] (Stadlmann’s criterion for this support at k=48) holds with our own test function. This is not a verification of 236: the analytic input (Stadlmann’s equidistribution estimates, with the corrections described in [8]) is Claimed and unverified by us.

The witness of Song and Yue, recomputed exactly

Appendix A of [18] prints the witness as 1295 integer triples (a,\alpha,c), P(z)=\sum c\,(\frac{26}{25}-s(z))^{a}m_\alpha(z) with monomial symmetric functions m_\alpha and a+|\alpha|\le25, used as P_U(t)=P(\frac{26}{25U}t) on the support above (U=\frac{521}{2000}, k=48). Appendix C prints reference integers I_{\mathrm{ref}}=(98!)^2I_0^{*} and J_{\mathrm{ref}}=(99!)^2(26!)^2J^{*}, where I_0 is the cap-free mass on \{s<U\}, J the capped numerator, and the stars denote the integer coordinates x=10^4t. The criterion is 48J/I_0>\frac{2001}{2000} (Literature (preprint), read in [18, §4.12, App. C]; that it implies \mathrm{DHL}[48,2] is Claimed). The authors write that a fresh evaluation reproduces I_{\mathrm{ref}}, while a fresh regeneration of J_{\mathrm{ref}} from the table “remains incomplete”.

All parts of every \alpha in the table are even. In our coordinates u=4t we have z=\frac{520}{521}u and \frac{26}{25}-s(z)=\frac{520}{521}(L-P_1(u)), and the augmented monomials A_\beta=|\mathrm{Aut}\,\beta|\,m_\beta satisfy p_rA_\beta=A_{(\beta,r)}+\sum_iA_{\beta+r e_i}. So P_U is, exactly, a polynomial in L-P_1 and the even power sums P_2,\dots,P_{24} (1311 terms, degree 25; code/songyue_convert.py, checked exactly at random rational points of \mathbf{R}^{48} by code/songyue_pointcheck.py).

Proposition 38 (Computed, exact rational arithmetic; the capped numerator by two independent programs). For the witness P_U of [18] and the support T above:

  1. (98!)^2\,10^{192}\,5^{150}\,521^{50}\,I_0=I_{\mathrm{ref}} exactly;

  2. (99!)^2(26!)^2\,10^{196}\,5^{150}\,521^{50}\,J_T=J_{\mathrm{ref}} exactly, where J_T is the capped numerator;

  3. hence 48J_T/I_0=1.000664921591\ldots>\frac{2001}{2000}, i.e. 4.0026596864\ldots in our scaling. With the capped denominator, 48J_T/I_T=4.0030988047\ldots (I_T/I_0=0.99989031). Without the caps, 48J_0/I_0=4.0036348167\ldots.

The factor 10^{192}5^{150}521^{50} (resp. 10^{196}5^{150}521^{50}) is what the change to the integer coordinates x=10^4t gives when Q=65125^{25}P_U (our reading of [18, Eq. (71)]; note 65125=25\cdot2605 and z=26x/65125), so items (1) and (2) are equalities of integers with 655 and 715 digits, not numerical agreements. Item (1) comes from a program written for this purpose, code/songyue_I0.py. It works in the monomial basis of the table: each pair m_\alpha m_\beta is integrated by summing over partial matchings of the parts, with a bitmask dynamic programme. It does not use the power-sum conversion. J_T in (2) is code/capped_check.py on the converted polynomial (56 minutes on four cores; code/cap6/check_sy236.json; comparison code/songyue_compare.py, output code/cap6/compare_sy236.json). J_0 is again in the monomial basis (code/songyue_J0.py). The values 4.0030988 and 4.0036348 agree with the capped and cap-free values claimed in [8] to every printed digit.

Second program. code/capped_exact.py (Proposition 27: cap-free value minus the correction integrals \mathrm{Corr}_1, \mathrm{Corr}_2, here 126 inclusion–exclusion pieces with m\le13, j\le3, each an exact rational, checkpointed piece by piece) was run on the same converted polynomial with the option –jonly (numerator only; about 32 core-hours, resumed from a checkpoint of five pieces). Its J_T equals the J_T of capped_check.py as an exact rational, hence also (99!)^2(26!)^2\,10^{196}\,5^{150}\,521^{50}\,J_T=J_{\mathrm{ref}} (code/songyue_compare2.py, outputs code/cap6/compare2_sy236.json, code/cap6/compare_sy236_exact.json); its cap-free I and J equal the monomial-basis values I_0, J_0 of songyue_I0.py and songyue_J0.py exactly. The two programs for J_T share only the input file: one sums over the exact large set, the other subtracts corrections from the cap-free value. The corrections are small: \mathrm{Corr}_1/J=2.3\cdot10^{-6}, \mathrm{Corr}_{2}/J=2.4\cdot10^{-4}, and the pieces with j=3 further coordinates above g are below 10^{-23}J in absolute value although they take almost half of the running time (15.3 of 32.2 core-hours, 47\%; runs/exact_sy236_jonly.log, code/cap6/pieces_sy236_jonly.jsonl).

So the capped numerator of [18], whose fresh regeneration the authors list as pending, is reproduced exactly by two independent implementations, and the variational criterion of both 236 preprints holds for their published witness. This is a statement about the variational step only. The equidistribution input ([18, Theorem 4.18]; Stadlmann’s estimates as amended in [8]) and the reductions from it to the variational criterion ([18, Thm. 4.33, §§4.12–4.14]; [19, Prop. 1]) are Claimed and not verified by us.

Corollary 39 (conditional; the analytic input is Claimed). The variational step of [18] is independently reproduced: the criterion 48J/I_0>\frac{2001}{2000} of [18, Prop. 4.21] holds for their witness, support and parameters at k=48, by two independent exact programs (Proposition 38). Together with the Lean-checked admissible 48-tuple of diameter 236 (boundedGaps_236_of_DHL_48), H_1\le236 then follows from their reduction [18, Theorem 4.33 and §§4.12–4.14] and their equidistribution theorem [18, Theorem 4.18], neither of which we have verified.

This is a reproduction of part of a claimed result, not a new bound. The project’s reproduced bound becomes 236 with exactly this caveat. The witness is theirs; our own degree-22 vector on the same support (Proposition 37, 4.0061) is also confirmed by two programs.

Remark 40 (an audit of the analytic input of 236; Claimed, audited inside this project, not peer review). We record the outcome of an adversarial check of the proof of [18, Theorem 4.18] (§§4.1–4.7, and its use in §§4.13–4.14), carried out inside this project by AI auditors, one per part of the proof, with exact-rational checks of every exponent inequality and a word-for-word comparison of the cited statements of Polymath8a [14] (notes/audit-sy236-thm418/, scripts in code/audit_sy236/). Its verdict: the theorem appears to follow from the cited literature after one local repair. The displayed bound (49) of [18, p. 27] for the correlation of two \mathrm{Kl}_3 sums, transcribed correctly from [14, Lemma 6.25, Cor. 6.26], carries the factor \gcd(g,m_1-m_2)^{1/2} with g=(r_1,r_2), r_i=gt_i; the prime-by-prime proof gives \gcd(g,m_1t_1^3-m_2t_2^3)^{1/2} instead (the local correlation is of size p exactly when the two local arguments coincide, where it equals \sum_a|\mathrm{Kl}_3(a;p)|^2). After this change the sum over m_1,m_2 has the same bound, and no exponent or margin changes. We re-ran the auditors’ numerical check (code/audit_sy236/kl3_coordinator_check.py, p=31, t_1=2, t_2=3): the normalised correlation is at most 1.03 when m_1=m_2 and at least 29.97 when m_1t_1^3\equiv m_2t_2^3\pmod p. The auditors also found that the swapped-factor step of Remark 42 does not occur in the proof of [18]. This does not change the status of 236: the audit is one AI reading per section, not expert review; the deep inputs (Deligne’s bounds, bilinear Bombieri–Vinogradov, Siegel–Walfisz) stay Literature; and §4.12, the Selberg main term and Lemma 4.32 of [18] were not audited, so the reduction [18, Thm. 4.33, §§4.12–4.14] as a whole remains unverified. The bound 236 remains conditional (Corollary 39).

One-sided certificates without the thin pieces

Exact equality of two programs is the strongest check, but the criterion itself only needs a lower bound for J_T and an upper bound for I_T. The following observation lets a one-sided certificate omit the most expensive pieces of Proposition 27(3).

Proposition 41 (Proved here; Bonferroni inequalities, folklore in substance). Part (1). In Proposition 27(3) write \mathrm{Corr}_1=\sum_{j\ge0}(-1)^jS_j and \int_VP^2=\sum_{j\ge0}(-1)^jS'_j, where S_j (resp. S'_j) is the sum over m of \binom nm\binom{n-m}{j} times the piece with j forced coordinates. Then S_j,S'_j\ge0, and for every t\ge0

\sum_{j\le 2t+1}(-1)^jS_j\;\le\;\mathrm{Corr}_1\;\le\;\sum_{j\le2t}(-1)^jS_j,
and the same for S'_j. In particular J_T\ge J-\left(S_0-S_1+S_2\right)-\mathrm{Corr}_2 and I_T\le I-S'_0+S'_1. A certificate of kJ_T/I_T>4 therefore needs only the pieces of \mathrm{Corr}_1 with j\le2, those of \int_VP^2 with j\le1, and all pieces of \mathrm{Corr}_2.

Part (2). Write \mathrm{Corr}_2=\mathrm{Corr}_2^{R}-\mathrm{Corr}_2^{U}, where, with W=\{u'\text{ admissible},\ U<R\},

\mathrm{Corr}_2^{R}=\int_{W}Q(u',R)^2,\qquad \mathrm{Corr}_2^{U}=\int_{W}Q(u',U)^2,
and expand each, as in Proposition 27(3), as \sum_{j\ge0}(-1)^jS^{R}_j and \sum_{j\ge0}(-1)^jS^{U}_j over the number j of forced coordinates. Then S^R_j,S^U_j\ge0 and
J_T\;\ge\;J-\left(S_0-S_1+S_2\right)-\left(S^R_0-S^R_1+S^R_2\right)+\left(S^U_0-S^U_1\right), \qquad I_T\le I-S'_0+S'_1 .
So a one-sided certificate of kJ_T/I_T>4 needs no piece with j\ge3, and no piece of \mathrm{Corr}_2^U or of \int_VP^2 with j\ge2.

Proof. Fix m and A=\{1,\dots,m\} and let G_A(u)\ge0 be the integrand of the piece (Q(u',R)^2 times the indicator of |u'|\le\tau and s_A>C_m for \mathrm{Corr}_1; P^2 times the indicator of |u|\le L and s_A>C_m for \int_VP^2). On the set where every coordinate of A exceeds g, the indicator that no other coordinate exceeds g is \prod_{i\notin A}(1-x_i) with x_i=1_{u_i>g}\in\{0,1\}. If N of the x_i equal 1, then \sum_{j\le\ell}(-1)^je_j(x)=\sum_{j\le\ell}(-1)^j\binom Nj=(-1)^\ell\binom{N-1}{\ell} for N\ge1 (and 1 for N=0), which is \ge\prod(1-x_i) for even \ell and \le it for odd \ell. Multiply by G_A\ge0, integrate, and sum over m and over the \binom nm choices of A; by symmetry the integral of G_A\,e_j(x) is \binom{n-m}{j} times the piece with j forced coordinates, which is \ge0. The last two inequalities are the cases t=1 and t=0 together with I_T=I-\int_VP^2 and J_T=J-\mathrm{Corr}_1-\mathrm{Corr}_2.

For part (2): In both cases of Proposition 27(3) (whether U=g or U=C_{m+1}-s_A), the region of a piece and the value of U are functions of u' that depend on the set A of large coordinates only through m=|A| and s_A (and on u' through R), not on the indicators 1_{u_i>g} of the other coordinates; given A, the integrands Q(u',R)^2 and Q(u',U)^2 are \ge0. So the argument above applies verbatim to each of the two parts: the truncation after an even level is an upper bound and after an odd level a lower bound. Use the upper bound at level 2 for \mathrm{Corr}_1 and \mathrm{Corr}_2^R and the lower bound at level 1 for \mathrm{Corr}_2^U in J_T=J-\mathrm{Corr}_1-\mathrm{Corr}_2^R+\mathrm{Corr}_2^U. ◻

Size of what is saved (Computed, from the exact pieces of Proposition 38, code/cap6/pieces_sy236_jonly.jsonl). On the witness of [18] the four levels of \mathrm{Corr}_1 are 2.3\cdot10^{-6}J, -1.7\cdot10^{-10}J, 7.0\cdot10^{-20}J and -2.5\cdot10^{-69}J for j=0,1,2,3, and the j=3 level took 7.1 of the 32.2 core-hours; all 46 exact pieces of \mathrm{Corr}_1 there, and the 96 pieces of \mathrm{Corr}_1 and \int_VP^2 of the second program for our own k=48 vector (code/cap6/pieces_kin48_D22r.jsonl), have the sign (-1)^j, as the proposition requires. For that vector the one-sided certificate of the proposition gives 48J_{\mathrm{low}}/I_{\mathrm{up}}=4.006081420807>4, 3.5\cdot10^{-12} below the exact ratio, and the omitted pieces took 4.3 of its 15.5 core-hours (28\%). The loss can also be bounded a priori (code/thin_bound.py): on \{|u|\le L\} one has 0\le R\le L and 0\le P_\ell(u)\le|u|^\ell, so |P|\le M:=\sum_i|c_i|L^{a_i+w(b_i)} and |Q|\le LM; and the region of a j=3 piece of \mathrm{Corr}_1 has C_m-mg\le r_A\le r_A+\rho\le\tau-(m+3)g, an interval of length \tau-C-3g=\frac{1}{250} for the caps of [8, 18], whatever m is. The resulting bound is 1.4\cdot10^{-14}J, and it is above all ten exact j=3 values. The same crude bound is useless for \mathrm{Corr}_2 (it gives 6\cdot10^{32}J): there the corresponding length is L-C-3g=\frac{8}{125}, sixteen times larger in about 44 dimensions, and the coefficient bound M is far above the actual size of |P| (cancellation in the power-sum basis). Both bounds above are for the witness of [18] (code/cap6/thin_bound_sy236.json); for a different polynomial, our own k=48 vector, we sampled |P| and found M larger by a factor of about 10^{28} (that sampling is not stored in the repository). Since the integrand of \mathrm{Corr}_2 has no sign, the proposition does not apply to it directly; splitting it into its Q(u',R)^2 and Q(u',U)^2 parts and applying the proposition to each gives part (2) of the proposition.

The one-sided certificate on the 240 vector (Computed, exact; capped_exact.py –bonf; files code/cap6/res_capfree_D20_bonf.json, pieces_capfree_D20_bonf.jsonl and compare_capfree_D20_bonf.json there). For the cap-free optimiser of Theorem 28 on the support of [19] (k=49), the 98 pieces that the proposition keeps give

49\,J_{\mathrm{low}}/I_{\mathrm{up}}=4.012038868083\ldots>4,
3.5\cdot10^{-8} below the exact ratio 4.0120389035705 of Theorem 28; J_{\mathrm{low}}\le J_T and I_{\mathrm{up}}\ge I_T hold as exact rationals, and every kept level has the sign (-1)^j. The two parts of \mathrm{Corr}_2 are each about 35 times larger than their difference (case U=C_{m+1}-s: 7.03\cdot10^{-2}J and 6.83\cdot10^{-2}J at j=0), but their levels still fall fast enough: the largest omitted level is of order 10^{-8}J, far below the margin 3\cdot10^{-3}. With the split, no piece with three forced coordinates is needed at all. This is a cheaper route to the same conclusion, not a new one.

Remark 42 (on [19, Lemma 5]; Literature (preprint) reading, not a result). Song and Yue [18, Remark 4.23] say that one step in the proof of [19, Lemma 5] lacks a hypothesis. We read the step in arXiv v1. Lemma 5 (Baker–Irving Type I) takes f=\alpha\star\beta with \alpha an arbitrary coefficient sequence and \beta smooth at scale x^\gamma. For \gamma\in(\frac12,\frac12+2\omega+\varepsilon] the proof applies Lemma 3 (Polymath Type II) “with the roles of \alpha and \beta swapped”. Lemma 3 requires its factor at scale \le x^{1/2} to have the Siegel–Walfisz property, and after the swap that factor is \alpha. Neither Lemma 5 nor case (I) of [19, Definition 9], where Lemma 5 is used, assumes this property of \alpha. So the step reads as [18] describe it. A natural repair is to check that the sequences \alpha produced by the decomposition do have the property, but we have not checked this, and we do not know whether the conclusion is affected. Our results on 240 and 236 concern only the variational step and are unaffected either way. The bound 240 may depend on this step, and so may the routes to 236 of [8] (which uses [19, Prop. 3]) and to 212 of [3]; we have not traced either. Our route to 236 goes through [18, Theorem 4.18] (Corollary 39), and the internal audit of its proof found no swapped-factor step there (Remark 40).

Remark 43 (the next rung, 186; Literature (preprint) reading and one Lean-checked fact). We read [13] and its numerical companion (the file short_gaps_numerics.pdf of the repository openai/PrimeGaps186). Their criterion is not a capped simplex ratio of the kind treated above. The sieve has one outer sum and, for each coordinate, two nested inner sums, and the criterion [13, Theorem 4.5] is a “signed minorant” inequality. It involves I=\|F\|^2, the inner masses J_{0,i} and J_{+,i}, and a residual E_{0,i} weighted by the negative part of Stadlmann’s prime minorant, at k=40. The test function lives on “fragment configurations”: each coordinate carries a Poisson process with intensity dv/v, so coordinate totals have Dickman densities. It is a 77-coefficient rational step function on a grid of 98304 cells per coordinate. Their Lemma 2.5 states \rho^*J^-/I^+>\frac{500103}{500000} and a loss bound L^+/I^-\le 696075110\cdot10^{-12} (from 97 components). We checked only the final rational arithmetic of their (2.34)–(2.35) (exact; it holds as printed), not any integral. The 40-tuple of [13, (1.3)] is admissible of diameter 186 (Lean-checked: boundedGaps_186_of_DHL_40’; the project already had another such tuple). Reproducing the variational step of 186 therefore requires a new evaluator: the fragment-measure forms, and the loss cover for the support failures. The pipeline of this paper does not cover it. We transcribed the forms I_H and J_{\lambda,H} of the companion and its table of 77 coefficients (notes/sources-186-forms.md). Two facts shape any reproduction. First, the cell masses of the Dickman densities are transcendental, so an independent check can only be an interval computation, not an exact rational one. Second, by symmetry everything reduces to one-dimensional arrays of length about 10^5, so the cost is a few thousand long polynomial products at a few hundred bits (our estimate). One formula of the companion, its layered cap convolution, is not specified completely in the text. The analytic inputs (a triply densely divisible variant of Stadlmann’s bilinear estimate, the prime minorant, and the exponential-sum bounds that are axioms of their Lean development) remain Claimed. We made a first independent check of one ingredient (Computed (floating point), 40 digits, not interval arithmetic; code/oai186_dickman_cells.py). On [0,3] the Dickman function has closed forms, \rho_D(u)=1-\log u on [1,2] and \rho_D(u)=1-\log u+\frac12\log^2u+\mathrm{Li}_2(1/u)-\frac12\log^22-\mathrm{Li}_2(\frac12) on [2,3], and so does \int\rho_D. With these we evaluated every cell mass of the seven grid caps of the companion (363{,}450 cells; code/oai186_dickman_cells.json). All lie inside the enclosures given by its recurrence and cell bound, as we transcribed them, and the enclosures have width at most 8.35\cdot10^{-10} in units of the cell. One transcription doubt (which of two bars is the upper one) turns out not to matter at this precision. This checks one input of the companion; it is not yet an evaluation of I_H or J_{\lambda,H}.

Proposition 44 (Computed, two independent programs, the second in ball arithmetic). Evaluate the forms of [13]’s companion, as transcribed in notes/sources-186-forms.md, with the layered cap measure of (2.15) read as follows (our derivation): split the retained 39 coordinates by the layer (b_{a-1},b_a] of their largest fragment over all seven cap values b that occur in the outer and face domains, and admit the erased coordinate in the outer shells whose cap is \ge b_a. Then

I_H=2.36853178533\ldots\cdot10^{-14},\qquad J_{\lambda,H}=9.0248755655\ldots\cdot10^{-14},
\frac{\rho^*J_{\lambda,H}}{I_H}=1.000206087\ldots>\frac{500103}{500000},
with J_0=9.00605408\cdot10^{-14}, J_+=1.91277\cdot10^{-16} and J_t=1.61669\cdot10^{-15}. Our I_H lies inside the enclosure [23685317816,23685317890]\cdot10^{-24} of [13, Lemma 2.5], and our J_{\lambda,H} is 5.9\cdot10^{-9} (relative) above its lower bound 90248755123\cdot10^{-24}.

How it was computed (code/oai186_IH.py, code/oai186_J.py, outputs code/oai186_IH.json, code/oai186_J.json; 7 and 23 minutes on one core). Nothing from the authors’ repository was used or run. By symmetry each form is a sum over the radial index r<n=98264 of exact rational weights against coefficients of products of one-dimensional arrays. M_{d,\eta}=\sum_{\pi}(d)_{|\pi|}P_0^{d-|\pi|}\prod_{B\in\pi}P_{e(B)} is summed over the set partitions \pi of the labels of the signature \eta; we checked this formula against brute force on small cases. The Dickman function was evaluated through its closed forms (logarithm and \mathrm{Li}_2), and the profile and Z in 110-digit floating point. The coordinate arrays were rounded down and up at scale 2^{-256}. All products are exact integer products by Kronecker substitution in GMP, not FLINT, which is the library of [13]. The erased-coordinate correlations are exact, and the final signed sums are exact integer sums. The result is therefore accurate far beyond the 10^{-9} of the comparison, but it is not a rigorous interval enclosure (the special functions are not interval-evaluated). A fresh-context audit of the derivation and both scripts found no defect. A second program (code/oai186_check2.py, combined by code/oai186_check2_combine.py; output code/oai186_check2.json) works in ball arithmetic throughout (python-flint arb, with the Dickman closed forms evaluated in balls) and obtains the coordinate sums differently, as m!\,[y^m]A(y)^d for the exponential generating series A(y)=\sum_jK_jX^j\exp\left(\sum_ey_et_j^e\right), instead of by set partitions. It gives I_H\in[2.36853178533302\cdot10^{-14}\pm3\cdot10^{-30}], J_{\lambda,H}\in[9.02487556547131\cdot10^{-14}\pm4\cdot10^{-29}] and \rho^*J_{\lambda,H}/I_H\in[1.00020608684\pm4\cdot10^{-12}], agreeing with the first program to 7\cdot10^{-17} (relative). Both programs use the same transcription and the same reading of (2.15), so they check the arithmetic and the code, not the reading; the agreement with the published values checks the reading.

What this does and does not show. The main-term quotient of [13, Lemma 2.5] is reproduced independently, and so is the reading of its layered cap convolution that we had marked as underspecified. Not reproduced: the loss bound L^+/I_H\le696075110\cdot10^{-12} (the 97 support-failure components of their §2.4), which the criterion also needs; and, as always, the analytic inputs (Claimed). The project’s bound does not move.

Source data and the bookkeeping of the loss bound (Computed, exact rational arithmetic; the full companion was supplied to us later). (i) From the rational definitions in [13, (1.13)–(1.19), (1.23)–(1.24)] we recomputed the two source ladders (29 and 43 rows, with order counts (12,12,5) and (12,12,19)), the retained rows (0–27 and 0–38) and the four boundary values \lfloor B/h\rfloor, the continuous caps and, through the grid rule, the integer cap masks (1.5) on which our I_H and J_{\lambda,H} rest (we had taken them as given), and the six group parameters with every guard of their Lemma 1.1. Every printed value is reproduced (code/oai186_support_data.py). The analytic comparisons in that lemma, with the distribution inequalities of [13], are not checked. (ii) For the 97 component bounds of their Tables 2.1–2.6 (code/oai186_tables_2_1_to_2_6.json) every Young inequality cA+B/c\le E and inner weighting, the six group totals, the total 696075110<697\cdot10^6, the component counts against their witness schedule, and the final margins (2.34)–(2.35) hold exactly (code/oai186_tables_check.py). This checks how the raw bounds are combined, not the raw bounds themselves; those come from the forms (2.16)–(2.21) of the companion, which we have transcribed (notes/sources-186-loss.md). (iii) Version 0.15 evaluates them for one inner group, I2old (7 components), in arb ball arithmetic (Computed, one program, high precision, not an outward-rounded certificate; code/oai186_loss_I2old.py, output code/cap6/oai186_loss_I2old.json). Every computed raw bound is at most its Table 2.3 entry and within 2.7\cdot10^{-6} of it in relative terms (within 2.5\cdot10^{-10} for five of the seven); for example 25776.59 against 25777 and 1511406796.9 against 1511410893, in units of 10^{-18}I_H^-. Two readings were needed that the transcription does not fix: the radial interval of the group, read as the span of old-ladder rows 12–23 (the alternative mask, base row 2 only, is 2–5\% low on every component and is ruled out), and the choice between the delay recurrence and the renewal majorant (2.3) for the low kernels: the delay recurrence gives values slightly below the table for L_2, L_3, the renewal majorant matches them to 2\cdot10^{-10}, so the companion apparently used renewal there; we inferred this from the numbers, and both choices give bounds no larger than the table. This does not check that the forms majorise the true failure integrals (their Lemmas 1.4–1.7, 2.3, 2.4), the completeness of the cover, or the other 90 components.

The raw loss bounds (Computed, one program per form family, arb ball arithmetic at 256 bits (inner) and 128 bits (outer); a high-precision re-evaluation of the published forms, not an outward-rounded certificate). The companion itself is now in the repository (code/third_party/openai186/, Apache-2.0), and with it the two pieces of group data that an earlier version had to infer: the radial intervals [13, (1.23)–(1.24)] (old-ladder rows 12–23 for I2old, as we had read) and the rule for the low kernels: the delay recurrence (2.1) only when M\ge64 and its acceptance tests pass (ordered intervals in [0,1], both endpoint sequences nonincreasing), the renewal majorant (2.3) otherwise. We implemented that test as stated. It selects renewal exactly for the two I2old bins where an earlier version had inferred it from the numbers, so that inference is now derived. For the clipping (1.26) we read the core and threshold of an inner row as b_t, C_t and of an outer row as a_t, A_t (its predicate in (1.21)). With this reading our schedule reproduces the counts stated in their Lemma 1.6: the first 11, 2, 5 order-5/2 low bins lie below every absorbed activation, and on the remaining 11, 5, 6 the order-three minimum is the group’s lower radial boundary. Programs code/oai186_loss_inner.py (the 45 inner forms (2.19)) and code/oai186_loss_outer.py (the outer forms (2.17) and (2.20)–(2.21)); outputs code/cap6/oai186_loss_inner.json, code/cap6/oai186_loss_outer.json. For the smallest low bins (fragment caps of a few cells) the Eulerian kernels use the closed form T_e=(\exp Q-X^{e+1}\exp(XQ))/(1-X) of [13, §2.1], which agrees with the direct sum to 3\cdot10^{-70} on a test case. Results: (a) all 45 inner components (groups I2old, I5/2old, I2new, I5/2new). Every value is at most its entry in Tables 2.3–2.6, and wherever the entry exceeds 10^6 (in units of 10^{-18}I_H^-) it lies within 1.6\cdot10^{-7} of the entry in relative terms (within 5\cdot10^{-10} for 33 of these 38). Rounding our values up and weighting them as in (2.31) reproduces the four inner group totals of their Table 2.7 exactly (55254, 435544, 1405159, 32422390 in units of 10^{-12}). (b) 33 of the 52 outer components. They are all twelve rank-two components and the third-factorial component of O_{5/2}, its low bins L_{16}–L_{22}, and the six rank-two components and the low bins L_4–L_{10} of O_2. Together with (a) they carry all but a fraction 1.2\cdot10^{-6} of the published L^+. Each R_G and V_G is at most its entry in Tables 2.1–2.2 and for the rank-two and factorial components it lies within 5\cdot10^{-9} of it in relative terms (for those of O_{5/2} uniformly 1.2\cdot10^{-9} below for A and 1.3–1.8\cdot10^{-9} for B). The largest gaps are in V_G for the low bins that use the renewal majorant: 3.4\cdot10^{-5}, 3.0\cdot10^{-6}, 3.6\cdot10^{-7} below the table for L_{16}, L_{17}, L_{18} of O_{5/2}, while their R_G agree to 6\cdot10^{-10}. A likely cause, which we have not checked, is the interval widening of the sliding sum in the companion’s renewal recurrence (2.3): it grows with the index, and V_G weights large indices of the erased coordinate. In every case our value is the smaller. With the published Young parameters c=q/10^6, our values give component bounds E whose sums over the finished components equal the table’s sums for O_2 and fall 3\cdot10^{-12} below them for O_{5/2}. The remaining 19 components (low bins of O_{5/2} and O_2) carry a fraction 1.2\cdot10^{-6} of L^+ in the tables; by Proposition 46 they could not affect the margin even if they were off by a factor of 10^4. Version 0.17 runs them (Computed, same program): at 128 bits the 15 smallest bins were rejected by the companion’s own test (“reject a nonpositive lower endpoint” in the renewal recurrence (2.3)), because the ball width of the sliding sum outgrows the majorant before it reaches 10^{-40}; at 256 bits all pass. All 19 values are at most their table entries; the largest relative gaps are again in V_G of renewal-branch bins (2.7\%, 1.0\%, 0.5\% below the table for L_{13} of O_{5/2}, L_3 of O_2, L_{14} of O_{5/2}, components whose bounds E are at most 14\cdot10^{-12}). With all 97 components, the component bounds E computed from our values with the published Young parameters sum to 38927521 and 622829238 for O_2 and O_{5/2} (table: 38927522, 622829241) and reproduce the four inner totals exactly; every raw value is at most its table entry (code/cap6/oai186_loss_summary.json). A fresh-context audit of both programs against the companion (items: clipping, slopes, bin lists, branch rule, closed form, masks and weights, P-sets and prefactors, rank-two and factorial arrays, the erased-coordinate correlation; notes/audit-186-loss/report.md) found no discrepancy. It flagged one reading as open: we take “the appropriate rows of (1.5)” to mean all face rows of the domain, so that for O2, I2old and I2new about 39 top indices of the next, smaller-cap row are kept. That reading is the one that matches the tables.

A second program for the decisive group (Computed, two independent programs). A fresh-context helper wrote code/oai186_loss_outer2.py from the companion alone, without opening the first program or its results until its numbers were final (notes/loss-outer2-report.md). It differs from the first program in arithmetic (exact integer polynomial products by Kronecker substitution in GMP, rounded at 2^{-192} with a running error bound, and mpmath at 320 bits for logarithms and exponentials, instead of Arb balls through python-flint), in the moment algorithm (a generating series in auxiliary variables for the power sums, expanded into exact integer coefficients and checked by brute force on tiny grids, instead of the set-partition sum (2.10); it reproduces the marking-ring identity (1.43), [xy]Q^{40}=40P^{39}C+1560P^{38}AB, and gives the analogue [y^3]=40F_0^{39}F_3+1560F_0^{38}F_1F_2+9880F_0^{37}F_1^3 for the factorial component), and in the cap rows (2.13) (prefix sums after a binomial expansion). It evaluated the 12 rank-two components, the third-factorial component and the low bins L_{19}–L_{22} of O_{5/2}: 99.95\% of that group’s published bound and 89.4\% of L^+. Every raw value lies below its Table 2.2 entry (by 1.2\cdot10^{-9} for A in the rank-two bins, as for the first program; the common offset is therefore in the companion’s production run, which can only make its bounds larger), and the two programs agree to 2.5\cdot10^{-14} (A) and 1.4\cdot10^{-12} (B) on the rank-two and factorial components and to 2.2\cdot10^{-9} on the low bins, inside the second program’s own error bound for the cell enclosures (2.2). The second program has no renewal branch, so the smallest low bins (with the inner groups and O_2, 10.6\% of L^+) rest on the first program alone; by Proposition 46, O_2 could be 33\% and the inner groups 10–230 times too small, and the unchecked low bins of O_{5/2} (0.05\% of the group) 2\%, without changing the sign of the margin above 1/50000. The ambiguous readings of the companion that both programs had to fix (barred d in (2.9) as point enclosures with the stated cap roundings, the radial mask of (1.25), the clipping (1.26) with c_t=a_t, V_t=A_t, the strict inequality in (2.18), the erased coordinate in (2.20)) were made independently and coincide.

Remark 45 (what this does and does not establish; the switch to the second phase). Now both numerical halves of the 186 criterion, the main terms I_H, J_{\lambda,H} and the loss bound L^+, have been re-evaluated by two programs each (for L^+ in the sense just stated). This is a re-evaluation of the companion’s computational forms: we have not checked its Lemmas 1.4–1.7, 2.3 and 2.4 (that the forms majorise the true support-failure integrals and that the cover is complete), its restoration step (Proposition 1.3, resting on the main paper’s Proposition 4.6), or any of the analytic inputs, which stay Claimed. So the project does not adopt 186, even conditionally; its bound stays at 236 (conditional). By the plan recorded in STATE.md (“switch as soon as rung 186 is finished”), the project now moves to its second phase: most of each run goes to the companion papers of Section 6, first the paper on the parity barrier, while rung 182 continues at a smaller share.

Proposition 46 (Computed, exact rational arithmetic on the published integers). Let \mu(L):=(\rho^*(J^--L)-I^+)/I^+ with \rho^*=2624989/10^7 and I^\pm, J^- the endpoints of [13, (2.33)]. With L=L^+ at the value of Table 2.7, \mu=2.336\cdot10^{-5}. The total loss may exceed its published bound by at most 1.84\% before \mu\le1/50000, and by at most 12.8\% before \mu\le0. The group O_{5/2} is 89.5\% of L^+, so by itself it may be at most 2.06\% (respectively 14.3\%) too small; O_2 and I5/2new may be 33\% and 39\% too small, and the other three inner groups by factors 10 to 230.

So whether the loss bound of [13] is right is decided by the group O_{5/2}, and within it by its 12 rank-two components and its third-factorial component (81\% of L^+). This is how we ordered the computation. We have not checked which of the two thresholds the restoration step of the main 186 paper ([13], its reference [8, Prop. 4.6]) requires (code/oai186_margin_budget.py).

Remark 47 (the last rung, 182; Literature (preprint), with Computed checks where stated). We read [16] (a 132-page bundle: the main paper and two analytic companions; notes in notes/sources-182.md). It claims \mathrm{DHL}[39,2] and hence H_1\le182 (its Theorem 1.2). Its framework is the fragment-measure framework of [13] on a grid four times finer (393216 cells), with four cap domains, a profile of 726 rational coefficients (the factor g of [13] and its 11 power-sum signatures times 22 cubic B-splines), a new five-prime minorant and a support restoration step. The claimed surplus before restoration is >2.7669\cdot10^{-4}, the restoration costs at most 2.1148\cdot10^{-4}, and the final margin exceeds 6.52\cdot10^{-5}. The analytic inputs (a new incidence Type II estimate and a new Type III estimate, proved in the companions) are Claimed; its Lean development takes 262 numerical inequalities as hypotheses. The top-level file of that development (shown to us later; not compiled here, since it needs Mathlib and modules we do not have) states H_1\le182 and \mathrm{DHL}[39,2] under four hypotheses: those 262 inequalities, two published prime-local bounds, a published rank-four Kloosterman bound (from which the new incidence Type II estimate is derived inside Lean), and a new Type III local Fourier proposition that the file itself calls an unproved input. The 39-tuple printed in [16, §12] is admissible of diameter 182 (Lean-checked: boundedGaps_182_of_DHL_39’; the project already had another such tuple). The 726 coefficients and the source-row tables of the restoration step are not printed in the bundle; they are in a separate repository that this project has not obtained. Jerry Kim supplied that data (the file TrialData182.lean; verbatim copy in code/data182/, with integrity checks: cell-aligned caps, knots, and the defining relations of all 98 source rows). From it we evaluated the denominator of the 182 criterion, I(F)=\|F\|^2, independently (Computed, one program, high precision, not a rigorous enclosure; code/sharifi182_I.py): splitting by the layer of the largest fragment over the five cap values 141720h<177528h<186253h<196608h<246256h, with the active coefficient vector on each layer the sum of the components whose cap is at least the layer value, gives I(F)=1.489634100811\cdot10^{-11}, inside the published enclosure [1.48963408009,\,1.48963412154]\cdot10^{-11}, at relative position 0.49993. The two cap numerators follow in the same way (code/sharifi182_J.py; the erased coordinate is capped by the smaller of its component cap and its outer-shell cap, and the retained configuration is split over all nine cap values that occur): J_0=5.6364590899\cdot10^{-11} and J_+=4.0824074297\cdot10^{-13}, each just above the published lower bound (relative excess 4\cdot10^{-11} and 5\cdot10^{-12}). With the published upper bound for the tail term, which we have not recomputed, these give a cap surplus q_H-1=2.76706\cdot10^{-4}, consistent with the certified \mu^-=2.76692\cdot10^{-4} of [16]. A second, independent program for I(F) (Computed, arb ball arithmetic throughout; code/sharifi182_check2.py) gives the rigorous ball I(F)\in[1.48963410081137215290\cdot10^{-11}\pm5.2\cdot10^{-40}], inside the published enclosure. It has its own parser of the Lean data, its own Dickman closed form (\rho(u)=1-\log u+\log u\log(u-1)+\mathrm{Li}_2(1-u)+\pi^2/12 on [2,3]), pointwise Cox–de Boor B-splines in exact rationals, and moments from the power series A(y)^{39} instead of the set-partition formula; it shares with the first program the reading of F and the layered decomposition over the caps, so it checks the arithmetic and the code, not that reading. The two programs agree to 1.7\cdot10^{-22} in relative terms; the first program’s value lies just outside the ball, which a test on a coarser grid traces to its fixed-point rounding at 2^{-224} (at 400 bits the difference drops to 3\cdot10^{-34}). The same second program was extended to the cap numerators (Computed, arb balls at 128 bits; code/sharifi182_check2_J.py, report notes/check2-J-182-report.md; row tables derived from the Lean shells and checked against the paper’s table, the J_+ mask taken literally as \chi_1(1-\chi_0), a brute-force self-test at k=3,4): J_0\in[5.63645908992367166973506\cdot10^{-11}\pm1.7\cdot10^{-39}] and J_+\in[4.08240742966433656776654\cdot10^{-13}\pm2.5\cdot10^{-40}], both above the published lower bounds (as exact rationals), agreeing with the first program to 2\cdot10^{-23} and 2\cdot10^{-32} in relative terms (the J_0 gap sits on the same three caps as the I gap and has the same size). Both programs share the reading of which caps form the layers and what the face mask in a layer is, which the source does not spell out. The tail term E_{H_C^c} ([16], (6.5)–(6.7) with H_C for C, and the step kernel K of (5.6), computed exactly in rationals; (5.7), 0\le K\le2.194, holds when the last block ends at n_0-1) is evaluated by two programs (Computed, code/sharifi182_tail.py, report notes/tail-182-report.md): T\in[5.28634880592807710064423118475\cdot10^{-13}\pm1.1\cdot10^{-40}], just below the published upper bound 5.286348805972699\cdot10^{-13} (relative gap 8\cdot10^{-12}). A second, independently written program for the tail (Computed, code/sharifi182_tail2.py, report notes/tail2-182-report.md) reproduces it. It has its own exact kernel, GMP fixed-point Kronecker products in the style of the first I and J programs with directed error bounds, its own B-splines, and a self-test (kernel check and brute force) with four deliberate mutations. It gives the enclosure T\in[5.2863488059280771006442311847462\cdot10^{-13}\pm3.2\cdot10^{-45}], which lies inside the first program’s ball; its exact upper end is below the published bound. Both programs share the reading of the source, the layered derivation of the contraction and the Lean parser (and the second author had seen the first program’s kernel function), so they check arithmetic and code, not that reading. The second program also quantifies the block-end ambiguity of (5.6), already noted by the first: with the nominal last block (r_{\max}=768\cdot512-1), (5.7) fails (K_{511}=2.19637) and the tail would exceed the published bound by 5\cdot10^{-7} in relative terms. So the Lean premise is consistent only with r_{\max}=n_0-1, the reading both programs use; the effect on q_H would be about 4\cdot10^{-11}. From the balls for I, J_0, J_+ and T the cap surplus is q_H-1=2.767059\cdot10^{-4}, above the certified \mu^-=2.766919\cdot10^{-4}; the tail costs 7.86\cdot10^{-5} of q_H, so the claimed final margin (6.52\cdot10^{-5}) would be lost if the true tail were about 1.83 times larger. The mass deficit \kappa_{\mathrm{true}} of the five-prime minorant ([16], (4.2)) was recomputed by two methods: our own reduction to three variables with Gauss–Legendre quadrature, and the paper’s exact simplex moment series after checking that the simplices tile the region (Computed, code/sharifi182_kappa.py, report notes/kappa-182-report.md). They agree to 58 digits: \kappa_{\mathrm{true}}=9.33070883240611992\ldots\cdot10^{-5}, inside the printed bracket and below \bar\kappa and \kappa_{\mathrm{cvx}}. The printed arithmetic of the restoration step was re-done exactly (Computed, one program, code/sharifi182_arith.py, report notes/arith-182-report.md; 86 checks pass): the constants of (8.3), the four Section 10 subtotals from the printed group totals, \bar L of (10.2), the margin of (10.3), and the inequalities of Sections 3.7 and 13.3. The bundle prints only group totals, so the 197 component integrals themselves cannot be checked from it. The 98 source rows of the trial data satisfy the printed row definitions exactly (30 checks, code/sharifi182_rows.py), and they give T_0=2491228441011/2624989000000 exactly. Still unchecked: the restoration integrals (2.115\cdot10^{-4} claimed in total) and the analytic inputs.

A floor for one-band hybrid supports (our own)

Polymath8b’s \varepsilon-simplex is the case A_1=1/4 without caps, so the following extends Theorem 15 to every one-band support of Stadlmann’s type.

Theorem 48 (Proved here for the reduction; Computed, interval arithmetic, for the table). Let T=T_k(\delta,(-\varepsilon,A_1),\underline B,\varepsilon) be a one-band support with \varepsilon\ge0 and A_1\le\frac14+\omega, with any \delta and any caps. If the criterion k(1-c_1)J(F)-kc_2K(F)>I(F) holds for some F supported on T, then M_k\left(4(\tfrac14+\omega+\varepsilon),4(\tfrac14+\omega-\varepsilon)\right)>4. This is false, for every 0\le\varepsilon<\frac14+\omega, in each of the following cases:

\omega 0 3/1000 1/300 7/1000 1/68
no k with k\le29 k\le28 k\le28 k\le26 k\le22

So, whatever \delta, the caps and the minorant, a one-band support cannot give \mathrm{DHL}[k,2] for k\le28 at Stadlmann’s level (\omega=0.003; the helper’s reconstruction of his conditions gives \omega\le0.0032), for k\le26 at the level \omega=0.007 of [3], and for k\le22 whenever \omega\le1/68, a range containing every \omega that satisfies the Type I condition 68\omega+14\delta<1 of [19, Lemma 5].

Proof. As c_1,c_2\ge0 and K(F)\ge0, the criterion implies kJ(F)>I(F). In the scaling u=4t, F becomes a function supported on L\mathcal{R}_k (because T\subseteq\{\sum t_i\le A_1+\varepsilon\}), the ratio is multiplied by 4, and J is the marginal functional over \tau\mathcal{R}_{k-1}; so 4<M_k(4(A_1+\varepsilon),4(A_1-\varepsilon)), and monotonicity in L and \tau replaces A_1 by \frac14+\omega. (For \varepsilon<0 one has \tau>L and the quantity is at most LM_k, which is covered by the case \varepsilon=0 below.) Monotonicity in k reduces each entry to the largest k shown.
